# AIDraw testing workflow

Last updated: 2026-08-13

## Source-control and macOS migration preflight

`npm run check:portability` inspects Git's tracked paths plus non-ignored prospective untracked paths without staging or rewriting them. It fails on case-fold or Unicode-normalization collisions, absolute/backslash paths, unsafe or reserved cross-platform components, platform-generated files, local profiles/caches/output, bootstrap credentials, and signing material. `.gitattributes` stores portable text as LF, preserves CRLF only for Windows controller scripts, and marks common binary assets; this policy does not authorize `git add --renormalize` or a bulk worktree rewrite.

The pinned Node 24 focused gate is `vitest run tests/main/portability.test.ts tests/main/release-metadata.test.ts tests/main/release-artifacts.test.ts tests/main/platform-services.test.ts`. It exercises synthetic collision/name/artifact/secret cases, the live Git CLI, release-host mismatch refusal, Forge target-architecture wiring, macOS Keychain/login-service policy stubs, and native package-layout metadata. It is launch-free structural evidence on every platform, not native acceptance by itself. Follow `docs/MACOS_DEVELOPMENT.md` for the current Apple Silicon evidence and remaining Keychain, release-signing, lifecycle, interaction, performance, hosted-run, and formal-QA gates.

macOS compatibility result (2026-08-09): pinned Node 24.14.0 inspected 359 tracked paths plus 5 non-ignored prospective untracked implementation paths with zero portability violations. The complete launch-free `npm run verify` gate passes typecheck, full ESLint, and 111 Vitest files / 615 tests. The unchanged 1,200 MiB performance budget's final run passes at 744.58 MiB after native-allocation remediation, and the exact local app/DMG/import/Keychain evidence plus explicit limitations are recorded in `docs/MACOS_DEVELOPMENT.md`. None of this replaces a clean hosted run or the independent workflow below.

This workflow defines three test levels for AIDraw. Every formal run uses an independent Codex task configured as **`gpt-5.6-luna` with `high` reasoning**. The implementer does not certify their own change.

Passing automated tests alone is insufficient. Every level includes:

1. source-level automated checks;
2. authenticated MCP behavior;
3. native Windows or macOS UI interaction, matching the packaged subject's host, through the Computer Use plugin; and
4. at least one cross-surface assertion where an MCP change is visibly verified in the UI or a UI change is verified through MCP.

Playwright is valuable automated coverage, but it does not replace Computer Use. Computer Use must target the real AIDraw window and exercise native menus, dialogs, focus, pointer placement, and visible rendering.

## Roles and invariants

- The primary task implements changes and selects the required level.
- A **new** Luna/high task performs each formal test run. Do not reuse the implementation task as the tester.
- On Codex desktop, the primary task also acts as the mechanical launch coordinator: it starts/stops the tester's declared isolated package outside the filesystem sandbox, but it does not perform or judge the test cases.
- The tester is read-only with respect to production source. It may run commands and write ignored test reports/artifacts, but it must not fix product code, soften assertions, or change tracker statuses.
- Test documents use a unique `QA L<level> · <UTC timestamp>` prefix. Never modify, save over, close, or discard a pre-existing user document.
- Every formal level uses the exact newly packaged executable with an isolated profile. Testing a convenient pre-existing AIDraw window is not a formal run.
- Packaging and formal QA run only on Node 24.x. The runtime preflight and post-package artifact verifier must both pass; a command exit code without an executable is a failure.
- Paid generation calls are never made during QA. Generation UI is tested with mocks, cancellation, denial, or validation-only paths.
- A missing UI window, unavailable Computer Use helper, unavailable MCP connection, or unknown build identity is **Blocked**, not Pass.
- If Computer Use encounters a confirmation-required destructive action, stop at the confirmation boundary. Never overwrite or delete user data merely to complete a test.

## Level overview

| Level | Name | When to run | Typical duration | Required result |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Smoke | Before handing off a bug fix or focused feature | 10–20 minutes | Core automated checks, one MCP edit loop, and one native UI edit loop pass. |
| 2 | Regression | Before merging a feature cluster or milestone build | 45–120 minutes | Full fast suite and packaged Playwright pass; core illustration, pixel, persistence, collaboration, and UI regressions pass. |
| 3 | Release exhaustive | Before every versioned release candidate | Several hours or longer | Release artifacts plus exhaustive MCP, UI, interchange, recovery, security, performance, and accessibility evidence pass with no open P0 defects. |

The automated commands are deliberately named `:auto`. They cover only the automated portion of a level; the Luna/high MCP and Computer Use report is still mandatory.

## Exact-build and engine identity preflight

Formal QA must never assume that a registered `aidraw` MCP connection and a visible AIDraw window are the same engine. Multiple packaged builds and isolated profiles can run concurrently. The first bootstrap trial demonstrated that this assumption can produce valid MCP results against one engine while Computer Use sees another.

The packaged Playwright suite may set `AIDRAW_E2E_AUTO_APPROVE_AGENT_CLIENT=opencode` only together with `NODE_ENV=test`, an explicit `--write-mcp-connection` file, and an `aidraw-e2e-*` user-data directory. Both the connection file and `AIDRAW_OPENCODE_CONFIG_PATH` must be children of that isolated profile. This narrowly scoped hook bypasses the native consent dialog and leaves start-at-login unchanged so CI can verify the real packaged OpenCode writer and result UI without touching a developer's global config or Windows startup state.

The packaged renderer-recovery regression may set `AIDRAW_E2E_RENDERER_RECOVERY=1` only with `NODE_ENV=test`, an `aidraw-e2e-recovery-*` user-data directory, and the fixed direct-child files `mcp-connection.json` plus `renderer-recovery-diagnostic.json`. The hook sends one fixed ≤1 KiB malformed event containing no artwork, job, prompt, task, token, credential, or path data. It cannot select an arbitrary diagnostic target or overwrite an existing file; every other profile retains the normal native Save dialog.

On Windows and macOS, packaged Playwright is a native Electron launch. `playwright.config.ts` calls the packaged-E2E boundary guard and refuses to run unless `AIDRAW_E2E_LAUNCH_CONTEXT=unsandboxed-gui` is present. That value is an operator declaration, not a sandbox escape or detector: set it only on the same shell call that is genuinely running with explicit unsandboxed/escalated user-session permission. Never set it inside the filesystem sandbox merely to bypass the guard. A Windows sandbox-child GPU exit such as `0xC0000135` / `exit_code=-1073741515`, or a macOS denial of native services/process inspection/DevTools startup, is invalid launch evidence; repeat the unchanged executable, arguments, environment, isolated-profile construction, and test through escalation before diagnosing the host or product runtime.

Example targeted packaged Playwright call (the whole call must use the shell tool's explicit unsandboxed/escalated permission):

```powershell
$env:AIDRAW_E2E_LAUNCH_CONTEXT = 'unsandboxed-gui'
$env:AIDRAW_E2E_OUT_DIR = 'out-agt06-lanes'
node scripts/npm-node24.mjs run test:e2e:only -- --grep "keeps four authenticated playback lanes visible and fair while a human draws"
```

The ordinary `test:e2e:only` and `test:level2:auto` commands select exactly the 27 self-contained packaged regressions. They discover the current Forge target by host and architecture (`AIDraw-win32-<arch>/AIDraw.exe`, `AIDraw-darwin-<arch>/AIDraw.app/Contents/MacOS/AIDraw`, or `AIDraw-linux-<arch>/AIDraw`), require that launcher and its platform-correct ASAR before collection, create only `aidraw-e2e-*` profiles below ignored `test-results/` or the system temporary root, and use graceful engine signals plus credential redaction before removing disposable profiles. Native primary shortcuts and additive selection use Control on Windows/Linux and Command on macOS.

Fourteen exact-checkpoint cases are manifest-routed to the retained suite and excluded from that clean-package default because they require a fresh externally declared profile plus exact executable/ASAR hashes. They are not skipped or weakened: select them explicitly with `node scripts/npm-node24.mjs run test:e2e:retained -- --grep "<exact retained title>"` and supply that case's documented declarations. Supplying one of the retained profile variables or an exact retained title to the older targeted command also routes to the retained suite for compatibility with frozen Windows controllers. FND-05 preserves one graceful crash-controller failure and a distinct corrected crash r2 PASS; alive-hang r1/r2/r3/r4 are separately attributed immutable failures and r5 is the distinct narrow macOS/arm64 current-package PASS. Every consumed identity remains non-reusable, and any future independently authorized run must use a fresh securely fused package plus the dedicated private wrapper and correlated direct-child profile/failure roots. UX-01 preserves its first graceful density-controller/evidence-security failure and its distinct narrow density r5 PASS; the fourteenth route now also preserves consumed window-chrome r1 as a separate pre-input coordinate-controller failure. Its corrected source route still requires wholly fresh correlated roots and a separate native lease before any retry. All private wrappers reject ambient credential-capable reporter output. `AIDRAW_E2E_SUITE=all` is reserved for a controller that supplies every required declaration; a clean Level 2 run must not manufacture or infer retained artifacts.

The first independent Apple Silicon Level 2 attempt at `test-results/luna-high/20260809T125847Z-macos-level2/automation-failure.md` is retained as a failed automation certificate: 0 passed / 37 failed / 0 skipped. Four cases tried the Windows EXE and returned `ENOENT`, 21 never obtained DevTools, one never attached headless Electron, and 11 failure reports cited missing retained-profile declarations (some failures shared a case/root cause). No renderer or product assertion ran, and no process survived. It remains immutable runner-failure history rather than product evidence.

The next independent Level 2 run is retained at `test-results/luna-high/20260809T134216Z-macos-level2/report.md`. Its exact automation passed (110 files / 608 tests and 27/27 packaged cases), authenticated MCP and native UI workflows broadly passed, and all eight documents retained substantive content across graceful restart. The overall certificate correctly remained **FAIL**: active tab/title/visible canvas, canonical `activeDocumentId`, and editor advisory disagreed before and after restart; an empty revision-0 document displayed another tab's transient path; and recovery marked all eight documents dirty, including the clean original and a successfully saved file. This is real product evidence, not a runner failure.

Local remediation now gives every workspace snapshot a monotonic revision, subscribes before renderer hydration, gates stale responses, keys canvases by document ID, cancels old-tab gesture commits/locks, rebinds advisory state only to the canonical active document, and persists ordered tabs/active ID plus each document's actual dirty/file state. Focused source coverage passes 5 files / 52 tests. The exact unsandboxed `node scripts/npm-node24.mjs run test:level2:auto` exited 0 with portability 359 tracked + 5 prospective paths, clean TypeScript/full ESLint, 111 files / 615 tests, package verification, and 27/27 packaged cases in 1.6 minutes. Its launcher is SHA-256 `8E1523B89F592FC77F85513E4AC21854E3F01E4BC87485CA1EAD9E7DEF201BDE`; its ASAR is `1B2C8E402F7226BFB6205429CFC4F20F1B0331E23E306EBD0E349D53CA28909D`. The strengthened case exercises rapid Bézier, held pixel-pencil, pending-lock release, selection-preview isolation, and an exact mixed clean/dirty eight-document graceful restart. A separate exact-profile headless probe independently restored the exact order/active ID, three dirty/five clean states, and a clean saved path.

The final independent Apple Silicon Level 2 report is retained locally under ignored evidence path `test-results/luna-high/20260809T154905Z-macos-level2/report.md` (not as a source-controlled artifact). It is a strict **PASS** with report SHA-256 `fa75d06855d80f77249312c43741172116bc688040290f5ab767f0e846aa0783`. The authoritative exact command exited 0 with 111/111 Vitest files, 615/615 tests, package verification, and 27/27 packaged cases. Native Computer Use plus two authenticated actors agreed on exact eight-document order, three dirty/five clean state, active/advisory identity, saved path/object, human pixel UI→MCP undo/redo, same- and cross-kind preview cancellation with zero locks, blank-original isolation, same-profile restart, native panels/dialog cancellation, and graceful credential-redacted cleanup. The certified launcher/ASAR and all five artifact hashes match the matrix in `docs/MACOS_DEVELOPMENT.md`. Level 2 is therefore earned for that immutable Apple Silicon subject; Level 3 and release certification remain unearned.

Use the supplied harness for every formal run:

1. Run the automated level through Node 24. On Codex desktop hosts, `node scripts/npm-node24.mjs ...` discovers the bundled Node 24 runtime; elsewhere, activate `.nvmrc` or set `AIDRAW_NODE24_EXE`.
2. The Luna task reports an `AUTOMATION_COMPLETE_AWAITING_COORDINATOR_LAUNCH` checkpoint containing only the executable path/hash and the planned isolated profile/connection/manifest paths. It does not spawn Electron.
3. The primary launch coordinator validates those paths are below the declared run root and starts the exact package with `scripts/qa-session.mjs`. On Codex desktop, this Windows or macOS native GUI command must use the shell tool's explicit unsandboxed/escalated launch permission and `--launch-context unsandboxed-gui`; launching Electron as a child of the filesystem sandbox can raise Windows exception `0x80000003`, lose the GPU child with `0xC0000135`, or prevent required macOS native services from operating before the health endpoint responds.
4. The coordinator runs `qa-session status` through the same explicit unsandboxed/escalated boundary on Windows or macOS, then resumes the same Luna task with its non-secret result. It must report `okay: true`, a live PID, matching native executable/profile identity, matching connection PID/URL, and an unchanged executable hash. A sandboxed macOS `ps` denial is not identity evidence.
5. Luna enumerates AIDraw windows with Computer Use. Read-only observation may be used to disambiguate candidates, but no input or QA document mutation is allowed yet.
6. Luna selects the window whose process-backed app identifier names the exact executable and whose Activity panel says `Listening at <manifest mcpUrl>`. The window, manifest, connection file, and process PID must agree.
7. Luna initializes the isolated connection with `scripts/qa-mcp.mjs`. Do not use the globally registered AIDraw MCP for a formal run because it may intentionally serve the user's normal profile.
8. Only after all identity assertions pass may the tester create or edit a QA document. Any mismatch is `BLOCKED`; request coordinator cleanup without mutating documents.

Connection and MCP state files contain a localhost bearer token. Keep them inside ignored `test-results/`, never print their contents, and never paste them into a report. `qa-mcp close` redacts/removes client state, and a successful coordinator `qa-session stop` redacts the stopped engine's connection file.

Example Level 1 bootstrap (replace the run ID once and use separate shell calls so each exit code is preserved):

```powershell
$AIDrawRepo = (Resolve-Path .).Path
$AIDrawRun = Join-Path $AIDrawRepo 'test-results\luna-high\<run-id>-level1'
node scripts/npm-node24.mjs run test:level1:auto
node scripts/qa-session.mjs start --exe "$AIDrawRepo\out\AIDraw-win32-x64\AIDraw.exe" --profile "$AIDrawRun\profile" --connection "$AIDrawRun\connection.json" --manifest "$AIDrawRun\session.json" --mode interactive --launch-context unsandboxed-gui
node scripts/qa-session.mjs status --manifest "$AIDrawRun\session.json"
node scripts/qa-mcp.mjs init --connection "$AIDrawRun\connection.json" --state "$AIDrawRun\mcp-state.json" --actor-name "QA Luna high L1 <run-id>" --actor-color "#7C3AED"
```

The coordinator owns `qa-session start`, `show`, and `stop`; those native-process operations run outside the Codex filesystem sandbox. Luna owns the sandboxed automated, status, MCP-client, Computer Use, assertion, and reporting steps. Write complex tool arguments to JSON files in the run root and pass `--args-file`; this avoids shell quoting from changing the MCP payload. At cleanup, Luna runs `qa-mcp close`, writes a draft result, and returns `TEST_COMPLETE_AWAITING_COORDINATOR_STOP`. After the coordinator stops the isolated engine and the harness redacts its connection credential, the same Luna task is resumed once to verify cleanup and finalize `report.md`. The harness preserves the non-secret profile/evidence; it does not delete test artifacts.

Normal `qa-session stop` never force-terminates a process. If a known native modal prevents the isolated engine from accepting its graceful quit signal, the coordinator may explicitly opt in with `qa-session stop --manifest <path> --force-on-timeout`. The command still waits for the normal 15-second shutdown first. Before force termination it must re-read the connection and live Windows or macOS process and match the manifest's executable path and SHA-256, PID, exact `--user-data-dir` profile, connection PID/URL, and loopback MCP endpoint. Any mismatch refuses the force action, and credentials are redacted only after the verified PID has exited. Never use this fallback for an unverified process or a non-isolated user profile.

## Common run protocol

Every tester follows this order:

1. **Identify the subject.** Record test level, UTC run ID, source revision if available, dirty/untracked state, Node/npm versions, executable path, executable SHA-256, and isolated profile path.
2. **Run the automated portion.** Use the exact Node-24 level command and preserve the full exit status. Confirm the post-package verifier named the expected executable. Do not summarize a failed command as passed because a narrower retry succeeded.
3. **Pause for coordinated native launch.** Luna returns the automation checkpoint; the primary starts the declared isolated subject through an explicitly unsandboxed `qa-session start` call, then resumes Luna. A native-service or process failure caused by a sandbox-child Electron process is an invalid test launch, not a product failure.
4. **Select the native window safely.** Use the Computer Use skill, initialize its runtime, read its guidance and confirmation policy, enumerate apps/windows, and continue only after the executable path and Activity MCP URL select exactly one intended AIDraw window.
5. **Establish the isolated MCP session.** Use `qa-mcp init`, join as `QA Luna high L<level> <run-id>` with a distinct color, list/observe before document mutation, and record document IDs/revisions.
6. **Exercise UI through Computer Use.** Observe, perform one state-derived action, refresh, and visually verify. Re-observe after every layout/modal/focus change. Use real pointer drags for drawing tests.
7. **Cross-check surfaces.** Verify at least one isolated-MCP-created change visibly in that exact AIDraw window and one Computer-Use-created change through an isolated MCP observation or revision diff.
8. **Record failures immediately.** Include expected/actual behavior, exact reproduction, affected document/revision, window title, visual observation, logs, severity, and likely tracker IDs. Continue only when later results remain trustworthy.
9. **Clean up safely.** Restore the initially active isolated tab, close/redact the QA MCP state, and close only safely saved QA documents. Return the stop checkpoint; the coordinator stops the isolated engine outside the sandbox and resumes Luna. Do not touch the user's normal AIDraw profile or use UI deletion as cleanup.
10. **Finalize the report.** Verify the isolated PID is stopped and use [testing/REPORT_TEMPLATE.md](testing/REPORT_TEMPLATE.md). `PASS`, `FAIL`, and `BLOCKED` are the only overall outcomes.

Reports belong under the ignored path:

```text
test-results/luna-high/<UTC-run-id>-level<1|2|3>/report.md
```

The tester also returns the concise outcome in its task so the primary task can read it without opening local artifacts.

## Level 1 — Smoke

Automated command:

```powershell
node scripts/npm-node24.mjs run test:level1:auto
```

Required MCP checks:

- authenticated `session_manage` join/inspect;
- list documents and record the initially active document;
- create one small QA pixel sprite with a unique name;
- observe structured state and PNG;
- apply one visibly animated exact-pixel transaction with a unique client operation ID;
- verify revision increase, nonblank PNG, attribution, and duplicate-operation idempotency;
- undo and redo as the originating agent;
- leave the session.

Required Computer Use checks:

- target exactly one AIDraw window and prove it is not blank or in the renderer recovery screen;
- open the dedicated New Document dialog and cancel it without creating an extra document;
- activate the QA sprite created through MCP and visually verify its dimensions and MCP-created pixels;
- select the pixel pencil, draw one clearly separate pixel with a real pointer action, and verify the visible cell matches the cursor location at a non-default zoom;
- exercise human Undo and Redo through native shortcuts;
- verify tabs, tool rail, canvas, Layers/Inspector/Activity panels, timeline, and bottom status remain responsive;
- cross-check the UI-created pixel through MCP observation;
- restore the original active document.

Level 1 passes only when the automated command, MCP checks, native UI checks, and both cross-surface assertions pass.

## Level 2 — Regression

Automated command:

```powershell
node scripts/npm-node24.mjs run test:level2:auto
```

Level 2 includes all Level 1 checks plus the following.

MCP and collaboration:

- exercise every public resource and tool without making a paid request;
- verify two independent sessions, per-actor undo, duplicate IDs, stale revisions, human-lock conflict, busy/backpressure response, cancellation/partial activity, trace inspection, and background-tab editing;
- test headless engine → attach editor → close editor → reattach lifecycle;
- test a new-path file approval in a disposable test root, denial/cancellation, timeout, and no unintended overwrite;
- verify recovery after a clean restart and explicit discard of an untitled QA document.

Computer Use core workflows:

- use native **File → Open**, keyboard Open, Save As to a new disposable path, and the close flow;
- create custom-size Illustration, Pixel Sprite, Pixel Tilemap, and Pixel Project documents from the dialog;
- illustration: select/multiselect, lasso, move/snapping, pressure/vector stroke, raster brush/eraser, path/node basics, all shapes, gradient, crop, text, eyedropper, layer operations, mask, alignment/distribution, boolean operation, opacity/blend/shadow/blur, Undo/Redo;
- pixel: pencil/eraser/fill/replace/line/shapes/wand/stamp/dither/lighten/darken/text/picker, selection, symmetry, wrap preview, palette edit/reorder, resize, linked and independent frames, duration, onion skin, loop/ping-pong, replay;
- tabs: overflow scrolling/all-documents menu, background activity badge, close/save behavior, and no resurrected discarded document;
- Activity: presence, named cursor, approval card, Stop All Agents, trace replay, and reconnect notice;
- verify no white screen, malformed-event crash, coordinate offset, or unreachable tab regression.

Current UX-04 evidence (2026-08-05): the exact `out-ux04-native` Windows package was identity-checked by executable SHA-256, PID, process path, isolated profile, and unique Computer Use window before native **File → Open** mouse activation. A disposable 321×123 `.aidraw` fixture was selected in the owned Windows file dialog and reopened visibly; authenticated MCP then asserted the active file path, persisted document ID, name, kind, dimensions, background, and clean state. No user profile or file association was changed.

Current UX-05 evidence (2026-08-05): `out-ux05-recovery` verified as a Windows x64 package with executable SHA-256 `97CB19FAAB85D2F64FF5F909CE24D056AAEE76FAE57ADB032D15A98B2195E716`. Its isolated packaged regression seeded synthetic embedded artwork, a waiting provider approval with prompt/provider data, declared task provenance, and live MCP credentials; then it reached the recovery boundary, saved and scanned the local-only diagnostic, proved renderer IPC plus authenticated MCP remained usable, and reloaded to the same canonical document/revision/artwork/job on the unchanged engine PID. The approval was never granted, so no provider request ran. The test exited the owned engine gracefully, and an exact executable-path process check returned zero survivors.

Current AGT-07 evidence (2026-08-05): `out-agt07-locks` verified as a Windows x64 package with executable SHA-256 `97CB19FAAB85D2F64FF5F909CE24D056AAEE76FAE57ADB032D15A98B2195E716`. One isolated packaged session held a real illustration-object move and a 16×16 sprite pencil gesture before pointer-up. Authenticated `session_manage:inspect` matched the held object ID and pixel region; overlapping `canvas_apply` delete/write calls each returned `locked`, the exact human-editing message, and `conflict.retryable: true`. Canvas hashes proved both human previews were visible while canonical state remained unchanged. Pointer-up committed only the human move and four index-1 pixels, authenticated `canvas_observe` matched, and occupancy cleared. Cleanup was graceful-only; the retained ignored profile removed its token after confirmed exit, and exact executable-path identity found zero survivors.

Current AGT-06 evidence (2026-08-05): `out-agt06-lanes/AIDraw-win32-x64/AIDraw.exe` is 225,614,336 bytes with SHA-256 `3C94648AD544F22F0539C658AE76D53CD510A9F11E21D2C6655B1EF84A5EEC59`. A controlled same-command comparison used the same package, Playwright scenario, arguments, environment, and isolated-profile construction. The sandboxed leg (`aidraw-e2e-lanes-HA0IB9`) failed before renderer startup after 31.7 seconds with GPU `exit_code=-1073741515` (`0xC0000135`) and `ERR_FAILED` for `aidraw://app/index.html`; the explicitly escalated leg (`aidraw-e2e-lanes-wbCFJv`) passed the full scenario in 10.1 seconds. This conclusively identifies the Codex execution sandbox, not AIDraw or the Windows host runtime, as the launch failure cause. After adding the fail-closed packaged-E2E boundary guard, an explicitly escalated acceptance rerun passed in 9.9 seconds and retained evidence under `test-results/retained/aidraw-e2e-lanes-nxBFck`: four nonoverlapping Lane 1–4 rows with distinct authenticated actors/colors/tasks all advanced; authenticated presence reported four working actors and later four idle actors with empty queues; agent operations committed at revisions 2–5; renderer and MCP agreed at revision 5; and the simultaneous held human preview changed visibly, dispatched in 283.078 ms, stayed noncanonical until pointer-up, then committed a 14-point human stroke. The exact PID exited gracefully, the connection record contains no token and reports `redacted-after-graceful-stop`, and an exact executable-path check found zero survivors. No Computer Use or desktop input was used.

Current AGT-05 evidence (2026-08-05): `out-agt05-background-tabs/AIDraw-win32-x64/AIDraw.exe` verified as a 225,614,336-byte Windows x64 package with SHA-256 `F47898F2C2067D289A9A5E69AC27CC5EA58DDBDFBCEA1B690E93B496ADD67582`. One isolated two-document session kept `AGT-05 Foreground board` selected while an independently authenticated `Background collaborator` animated a 600-point stroke in `AGT-05 Background board`. The background tab showed an actor-colored `active` badge carrying live `pen` coordinates that matched authenticated working presence; a simultaneous real foreground pointer gesture dispatched in 239.855 ms, changed the held-preview hash, remained noncanonical until release, and committed a 13-point human stroke before the agent settled. Only an explicit tab activation changed focus, where the production named cursor label rendered at 117.25×18 px in the actor color; returning to the foreground reproduced the exact committed canvas hash. After the agent committed background revision 1, the foreground remained active and visually identical, the background tab became unread `complete`, and presence returned idle with queue depth 0. Authenticated `canvas_observe` matched both revision-1 documents, the background agent stroke, and the foreground human stroke. Retained profile `aidraw-e2e-background-tabs-thKhUZ` contains four visual/evidence artifacts; PID 44652 exited gracefully, the exact package has zero survivors, both owned attempt profiles are token-free and redacted, and a recursive secret scan returned no matches. No Computer Use, desktop input, screen control, or force termination was used.

Current QA-06 file-approval evidence (2026-08-05): `out-qa06-file-approval/AIDraw-win32-x64/AIDraw.exe` retained SHA-256 `F47898F2C2067D289A9A5E69AC27CC5EA58DDBDFBCEA1B690E93B496ADD67582`. Windows npm/cmd mangled two anchored grep selectors; both returned `No tests found` before launch and left process/profile state absent. An explicitly escalated read-only list then proved `--grep=QA-06` selects exactly one test, and the same hash/profile/process preflight plus that selector passed 1/1 in 4.6 seconds. The authenticated approval card showed the exact actor, path, and Deny/once/session/persistent choices. Denial preserved revision-1 canonical renderer/MCP state and created no target; a separate allow-once job wrote only `allowed-once-agent-save.aidraw` (3,147 bytes); a third same-folder request still required approval and was denied. Evidence records cancelled/completed/cancelled jobs, `denialUnchanged` and `rendererMatchedMcp`, zero paid/provider requests, graceful exit code 0, and `redacted-after-graceful-stop`. The exact executable has zero surviving processes; retained connection/evidence contain no bearer, Authorization, or token key; `trusted-folders.json`, denied target, and no-trust-probe target are absent.

Current QA-06 animated-sprite evidence (2026-08-05): `out-qa06-animated-sprite/AIDraw-win32-x64/AIDraw.exe` remained 225,614,336 bytes with SHA-256 `F47898F2C2067D289A9A5E69AC27CC5EA58DDBDFBCEA1B690E93B496ADD67582`. The first isolated run is preserved as failed acceptance evidence: export completed and wrote the bounded APNG, but the test incorrectly expected the privacy-redacted authenticated `job_manage` summary to disclose canonical `result.output`. Headless malformed-PDF object/stream follow-up (2026-08-07): a fixed-date local `pdf-lib` source with one tiny Flate content stream is byte-length-preservingly mutated into three deterministic cases: a missing catalog root, a missing page-tree kid, and a missing `endstream`. Real pdf.js 6.2.108 rejects them respectively during document load, page lookup, and render; every loading task is destroyed and no import result escapes. Source audit exposed one nondeterministic error-precedence defect: if the primary malformed-input operation and `loadingTask.destroy()` both rejected, JavaScript `finally` semantics replaced the useful pdf.js error with the cleanup error. Import now records both outcomes explicitly, always awaits destruction, preserves the primary parser/page/render error when both fail, and still fails closed on a destruction-only error after otherwise successful work. The focused importer independently replays 27/27, and the exact PDF/import/export/utility suite passes 58/58 with clean typecheck and targeted lint. pdf.js remains the recovery authority: malformed `endobj`, Flate-header, parent-reference, and stream-length cases observed to recover were deliberately not rejected by new AIDraw policy. The production utility import preserves the fractional 240.746×150.746 pt page as an exact 241×151 illustration, returns an explicit raster-fallback/re-export warning, retains exactly `LibreOffice PDF`, `CLIPPED TRANSPARENCY`, and `external producer fixture` on a hidden editable-text layer tagged with the typed `pdf-extracted-text` interchange role, and renders a fully opaque fallback. The adjacent production change makes only that importer-authored role eligible for hidden PDF export: each supported run is emitted once at opacity zero with an explicit Helvetica-substitution/retention report, an otherwise identical untagged hidden layer emits no text, and an unsupported `漢` run is omitted with an exact loss warning while the other two remain searchable. pdf.js extracts all three supported phrases from the normal AIDraw re-export, whose production re-import remains 241×151, opaque, and within the same visual thresholds. An independent Poppler 26.05.0 audit also extracts all three phrases and measures the re-export at 6.694/255 mean channel delta with 4.472% of channels above delta 32 against the original golden; its raster remains visibly intact but is not claimed as vector content. The final focused Node 24.14.0 test passes 2/2; portability passes for 364 tracked + 5 prospective paths, TypeScript and full ESLint are clean, and the complete launch-free collection passes 112 files / 617 tests including all 32 MCP contract cases. This is only the exact one-page supported-text LibreOffice clipped-alpha import/re-export fixture, not arbitrary Unicode/font retention, editable PDF operators, other producer/version/page families, packaged/native behavior, PSD fidelity, or broad PDF compatibility.

Headless recovery-journal teardown evidence (2026-08-07): the prior full gate's post-test `ENOENT` rename was a fixture lifecycle race, not a journal atomicity defect. `DocumentService.initialize()` schedules a recovery compaction, but `transaction-policy.test.ts` removed its owned temporary root without first awaiting the same `flushRecovery()` barrier used by production shutdown. The fixture now tracks every service, settles all recovery flushes before removing any root, and reports every flush or cleanup rejection through one `AggregateError`; genuine write/rename failures are not swallowed. A deterministic journal regression starts compaction without awaiting it and proves `flush()` remains pending until the snapshot rename completes and is readable. The exact journal/transaction pair independently replays 8/8, broader journal/transaction/document-service/persistence coverage passes 32/32, and the single subsequent full default Node-24 replay passes 108 files / 584 tests with exit 0. A bounded follow-up audit found exactly seven test files with direct `DocumentService` construction: transaction-policy was already corrected; MCP contract fixtures were already safe because `McpHost.stop()` flushes before their roots are removed; and document-service, playback-scheduler, trace-store, batch-manager, plus generation-manager integration owned roots without the explicit barrier. All eighteen constructors in those five files are now tracked, flushed before removal, and retain aggregate flush/cleanup error reporting. The exact seven-file lifecycle suite passes 38/38 with clean typecheck and targeted lint. No journal scheduling, ordering, durability, atomic-write, or cleanup policy changed, and no second full-suite replay was needed.

QA-08 aggregate Tiled cell-budget design checkpoint (2026-08-07): the existing per-layer ceiling is 4,194,304 cells and the aggregate ceiling is 16,777,216, so an exact accepted fixture necessarily retains four maximum decoded JavaScript arrays before canonical construction. The production layer builder then maps each maximum layer into 4,194,304 `{x,y,gid}` change objects before sparse writing. A launch-free pinned-Node-24 measurement at 1,048,576 cells observed an 8.14 MiB heap / 8.47 MiB RSS data-array increase in 2.18 ms and a further 56.18 MiB heap / 77.24 MiB RSS change-object increase in 52.9 ms. Linear scaling gives a conservative lower-bound projection of about 130 MiB retained data heap plus 225 MiB heap / 309 MiB RSS for one maximum change array, before the 16 MiB decode buffer, parser, canonical document, and garbage-collection overlap; practical peak therefore exceeds roughly 350 MiB heap and 450 MiB RSS. One maximum zero-GID binary layer is 16,777,216 raw bytes but compresses to 16,316 bytes / 21,756 base64 characters, confirming disk size is not the limiting factor. The unchanged focused 51-test gate completes in 1.98 seconds. No exact-boundary test or product change is claimed: forcing this corpus into the fast suite would be disproportionate, and extracting a private numeric seam would not prove production import/canonical behavior. The safe follow-up is an isolated opt-in child-process gate with an explicit high-memory host preflight, bounded timeout, test-owned temporary root, four 2,048×2,048 layers for exact acceptance, and a separate 16,777,217-cell rejection case; any two-pass pre-decode geometry policy must remain a separately authorized error-ordering decision.

Headless native checkpoint index/payload/revision reconciliation evidence (2026-08-12): one writer-produced archive reopens an exact main illustration plus two ordered checkpoints with no warnings. The boundary is deliberately strict for fields that drive ordering, renderer attribution, agent deletion authorization, and restore diagnostics: selected summary and payload metadata must independently match the seven-field writer contract, agree exactly with one another, and bind the migrated checkpoint document's identity and revision to `sourceRevision`. Bad auxiliary entries remain warning-isolated so valid artwork and sibling checkpoints are recoverable. Persisted names remain nonblank without inheriting the 80-character manual-entry cap because repeated restore-generated safety names can legitimately grow; arbitrary repair or normalization was rejected. Focused checkpoint policy/persistence/service/merge/MCP coverage passes 5 files / 70 tests. The complete pinned Node-24 `verify` gate passes portability for 369 tracked plus 23 prospective paths, TypeScript, full ESLint, and 122 Vitest files / 727 tests. Headless FND-03/QA-08 persisted-pixel integrity evidence (2026-08-12): pixel migration intentionally normalizes incomplete agent-authored and older records, but previously returned that normalized value without validating it. Nested chunk bytes, tile definitions, collisions, Wang terrain, map objects, libraries, links, and conversion settings could therefore remain malformed after recovery and reach renderer/export code; different raw records carrying one entity ID could also overwrite one another during rekeying. The boundary is now explicitly two stage. Recovery still supplies missing frame/cel entity metadata and empty chunks, canonicalizes recoverable record-key aliases, defaults absent reusable-library/link/conversion preferences, drops records that cannot identify a sprite exposure, and preserves unavailable linked-cel/group/asset/cache targets. Duplicate raw asset/layer/frame/cel IDs reject before overwrite. The normalized result must then pass strict shared palette/library/link/conversion, sprite/layer/frame/cel/chunk, tileset/tile/animation/collision/Wang, and tilemap/layer/object/chunk schemas; new pixel asset/library operations use the same nested contracts with stronger live-reference checks.

A representative core project retains exact indexed and GID chunks, two sprite frames, a missing linked-cel target, a frame palette, tile animation/collision/Wang metadata, a polygon map object, a missing linked cache, libraries, and conversion settings. Rekeyed asset/layer/frame/cel controls canonicalize to their entity IDs, while an absent-preference twin receives the established defaults. Nineteen mutations independently reject malformed/duplicate palette and library state, invalid or duplicate links, conversion settings, duplicate normalizable identities, short or noncanonical base64, contradictory chunk keys/origins and expanded chunks, duplicate cel exposure, tile identity, Wang colors, map-object geometry, and document scope. New-operation controls reject the same malformed chunk and duplicate stamp/cycle IDs. A writer-produced native project reopens with exact pixel, GID, and map-object semantics; five CRC-valid `document.json` twins inject malformed palette/chunk/tile/object or duplicate-asset identity and reject before canonical return. Direct invalid save leaves the prior archive byte-exact and removes its temporary sibling. Recovery excludes a malformed pixel snapshot without suppressing its valid sibling or inferring the invalid active ID. Chunk payloads are canonical base64 for exactly 1,024 indexed bytes or 4,096 GID bytes, and keys agree with aligned bounded origins. Tileset count limits reuse the established Tiled importer ceilings; no new resource values were introduced.

Focused schema/migration/native/recovery coverage passes 4 files / 63 tests; the broader reducer, animation, Tiled/raster import, render/export, fragment, generation, utility, and live-MCP compatibility selection passes 32 files / 303 tests. The complete pinned Node-24 `verify` gate passes portability for 369 tracked plus 23 prospective paths, TypeScript, full ESLint, and 122 Vitest files / 747 tests. The performance gate passes 1/1 at 756.7 MiB RSS growth with native save at 124.82 ms and every timing under budget; `/private/tmp/aidraw-pixel-schema-performance-gate.json` is not retained release evidence. That schema-only checkpoint did not yet reconcile every missing cross-record target or stored palette index; the adjacent palette-reference checkpoint closes only the palette-length and frame-override-order gap. It does not authenticate linked content, prove arbitrary corrupt-document repair, exercise packaged UI or broader platforms, create Level 3 evidence, or establish RC readiness.

Headless FND-03/PIX-02/QA-08 palette-reference integrity evidence (2026-08-12): strict pixel shapes previously allowed a canonical sprite-cel byte or reusable-stamp cell equal to or greater than the current palette length, and allowed a frame override with a different palette length or entry-ID order. Those values could reach rendering as the magenta missing-color diagnostic even though that diagnostic is not an authored palette color. Persisted native, recovery, and utility-imported state now validates every cel chunk and stamp against the current palette after normalization and exact shape checks. Frame overrides must match its length and entry IDs in order, though their colors may differ. Live cel-set, cel-region, stamp-replace, frame-add, asset-add, and asset-replace operations enforce the same rules against the palette at their sequential reducer position, so a transaction may deliberately expand a palette before using its new slot.

Deterministic controls mutate a canonical chunk byte, stamp cell, and frame override; inject a CRC-valid bad native `document.json`; reject an invalid direct save without changing its destination or leaving a temporary sibling; and isolate one corrupt recovery snapshot while a valid sibling remains available. Seven live operation variants reject before mutation, and the source document remains exact. Deleting a frame removes its frame-specific override, while the generated inverse restores that customized palette exactly. A prior checkpoint-isolation sentinel was corrected from out-of-range index 17 to the valid last default slot, index 15, because that test concerns checkpoint identity rather than corrupt pixel state. Focused coverage passes 4 files / 65 tests; the broader reducer, import, render/export, generation, utility, fragment, and MCP producer/consumer selection passes 26 files / 239 tests. The complete pinned Node-24 `verify` gate passes portability for 369 tracked plus 23 prospective paths, TypeScript, full ESLint, and 122 Vitest files / 749 tests. Headless FND-09/SEC-05/QA-08 generic-image admission evidence (2026-08-12): an attempted main-process decoder reuse proved unsafe when a 24-byte header-valid truncated PNG terminated a Vitest worker and isolated pinned-Node probes exited with signal-derived status 139 instead of returning a rejection. Production admission now performs only pure canonical base64, declared-length, SHA-256, PNG/APNG/JPEG/WebP/GIF MIME/header, display-geometry, 8,192-side, and 16MP checks in the main process. The existing 1.5 MB inline ceiling and 128 MiB native ZIP binary-entry ceiling are reused. A typed `validate-image` request then performs the real codec decode only in the supervised raster worker; contradictory results retire that worker, a decoder exit rejects the active request, and queued work restarts fresh. A separate unsafe-geometry control rejects before any worker starts.

The production engine injects that validator into live `asset.add` and native open/save, and the packaged CLI owns a lazy supervisor for native reads. Deterministic controls prove accepted live data commits, decoder rejection leaves the document unchanged, native open retains metadata but omits undecodable data with exact `Embedded data for asset “Integrity asset” is not a valid decodable image and was ignored.`, and CLI export follows the same warning-and-omission recovery policy. Save rejection occurs before lazy preview rendering, temporary creation, or destination replacement; the prior archive remains byte-exact. Warning-and-omission was retained over whole-document rejection because it matches established missing/corrupt-payload recovery, while in-process decode was rejected because termination cannot be caught. Focused typecheck/ESLint and 7 files / 81 tests pass; a broader utility/result-policy/import/export/render/generation/native/CLI selection passes 17 files / 124 tests. The complete pinned Node-24 `verify` gate passes portability for 369 tracked plus 25 prospective paths, TypeScript, full ESLint, and 122 Vitest files / 753 tests. The performance gate passes 1/1 at 757.44 MiB RSS growth with native save at 129.26 ms and every metric under budget; `/private/tmp/aidraw-supervised-image-validation-performance-gate.json` is temporary and not retained release evidence. The non-certifying audit remains Level 3 `BLOCKED` 2 and stable v1 `BLOCKED` 5, with 25 P0 rows unfinished, 59 selected-v1 P1 rows unclosed, and 79 dirty paths. At that checkpoint, recovery-journal and utility-import-result asset reconciliation remained open; the follow-up below closes only data-bearing recovery and embedded-result payload admission. A retained hostile decoder fixture, exact packaged behavior, broader codec compatibility, native-platform evidence, Level 3, and RC readiness remain unclaimed.

Headless FND-07/FND-09/QA-08 imported-and-recovered image-payload evidence (2026-08-12): utility-import result documents now pass complete generic-asset metadata plus every embedded payload through the established canonical base64, declared length/SHA, PNG/APNG/JPEG/WebP/GIF MIME/header, 128 MiB, display-geometry, 8,192-side, and 16MP envelope checks. The import promise remains unresolved while each distinct payload enters the typed supervised `validate-image` lane. Pure malformed output retires the producer; if the decoder process exits, the import returns no document and a later request starts in a fresh worker.

Recovery uses the same payload contract through the production-injected decoder but deliberately has different loss semantics. A fixed temporary journal contains two valid metadata records: one envelope-valid PNG rejected by the injected decoder and one SHA-contradictory copy. Recovery returns the exact document and active identity, preserves its real dirty state and both asset metadata records, removes both `data` fields, invokes the decoder only for the envelope-valid source, and publishes exactly one aggregate warning. A renderer bootstrap regression proves that warning becomes one warning toast; ordinary workspace reconciliation does not create a separate warning channel. Whole-import rejection was retained because utility output has not entered user state. Metadata-preserving omission was chosen for recovery because rejecting an otherwise valid unsaved document would discard more work, while silent omission was rejected because missing artwork requires visible notice. Metadata-only recovery entries, immediate journal rewriting, a retained hostile decoder fixture, arbitrary codecs, packaged warning presentation, broader PDF/PSD/image compatibility, Level 3, and RC readiness remain unclaimed.

Headless MAP-12/QA-02/QA-03 Tiled-transform evidence (2026-08-12): the former duplicated Canvas transform chain produced the main-diagonal image for diagonal-only and the anti-diagonal image for diagonal-plus-both-flips, reversing those two Tiled meanings on a labeled square tile. Headless FND-03/QA-08 persisted-illustration integrity evidence (2026-08-12): schema-2 migration previously required illustration layer/object records only to be object-like values carrying a string `type`, plus a few manual paint-cache checks. Malformed transforms, paints, text ranges, image crops, filters, artboards, membership arrays, brush presets, guides, and snap settings could therefore survive native/recovery migration and reach recursive renderer/export consumers even though live operations already rejected those same shapes. Persisted illustration state now reuses the established artboard, group/vector/paint layer and stroke, vector-stroke/path/shape/text/image/group object, brush, guide, snap, and animation schemas. Root/member IDs are unique, and layer/object record keys must match their entity IDs.

A core control exercises exact object/brush/guide state while deliberately retaining a missing root layer, vector member, layer mask, and object mask. Twelve mutations independently reject an invalid artboard, duplicate root order, non-record layer map, layer key/ID contradiction, expanded layer, invalid paint pressure, object key/ID contradiction, nonfinite transform, expanded object, duplicate brush/guide IDs, and expanded snap settings with exact field-family errors. A writer-produced native control reopens one exact shape; four CRC-valid `document.json` twins inject invalid artboard/order/layer/object forms and reject before canonical return. Direct invalid-object save leaves the prior archive byte-exact and removes its temporary sibling. A corrupt recovery snapshot is excluded without suppressing its valid sibling or inferring the invalid active ID. Whole-document rejection was retained for malformed canonical records; missing references and valid unreferenced records remain recoverable, and existing absent-preference defaults remain compatible. Focused schema/persistence/recovery coverage passes 3 files / 55 tests; broader reducer/migration/native/recovery/illustration-producer compatibility passes 19 files / 235 tests. The complete pinned Node-24 `verify` gate passes portability for 369 tracked plus 23 prospective paths, TypeScript, full ESLint, and 122 Vitest files / 742 tests. The performance gate passes 1/1 at 750 MiB RSS growth with native save at 126.76 ms and every timing under budget; `/private/tmp/aidraw-illustration-schema-performance-gate.json` is not retained release evidence. This does not reconcile every reference, reject dangling targets, complete persisted pixel-family validation, validate asset MIME/decode semantics, exercise packaged UI or broader platforms, create Level 3 evidence, or establish RC readiness.

Headless FND-03/QA-08 persisted-attribution integrity evidence (2026-08-12): the canonical document schema previously admitted `activity` and `provenance` as arrays of `unknown`. A malformed activity actor could therefore survive native read and reach document-open agent accounting or renderer controls that dereference actor fields; malformed provenance source lists could reach asset-reference traversal. The shared migration boundary now requires every activity entry to use the exact record envelope, a schema-valid actor/status, nonempty identity/label/timestamp fields, a finite nonnegative safe operation count, and optional string details. Provenance reuses the strict live-operation provider/model/prompt/source/mask/timestamp/conversion schema. Duplicate activity or provenance IDs reject deterministically.

One core baseline preserves exact valid records even when provenance deliberately names missing assets, proving the existing partial-reference recovery behavior. Ten mutations cover invalid actors/status/counts, expanded activity/provenance envelopes, invalid provider/source/conversion shapes, and duplicate IDs with exact activity- or provenance-specific errors. A writer-produced native archive then reopens exact attribution, while three CRC-valid `document.json` variants independently inject a null actor, a scalar source list, or a duplicate provenance ID and reject before canonical return. Directly saving an invalid activity record fails validation before destination replacement, leaves the existing archive byte-exact, and cleans its temporary sibling. Whole-document rejection was retained because these records are canonical document state used by attribution and reference consumers; silently dropping them would invent history-loss semantics. Missing referenced payloads remain recoverable. Focused schema/persistence coverage passes 2 files / 40 tests; broader migration/persistence/service/recovery/import/generation coverage passes 8 files / 93 tests. The complete pinned Node-24 `verify` gate passes portability for 369 tracked plus 23 prospective paths, TypeScript, full ESLint, and 122 Vitest files / 738 tests. The unchanged performance gate passes 1/1 at 749.3 MiB RSS growth with every timing under its budget; `/private/tmp/aidraw-attribution-performance-gate.json` is not retained release evidence. This structurally validates file-authored attribution but does not authenticate it, prove provider activity, add signatures, validate every canonical entity family, exercise packaged UI or broader platforms, create Level 3 evidence, or establish RC readiness.

Headless FND-07/QA-08 recovery-integrity evidence (2026-08-12): document IDs are opaque canonical strings, and native/utility imports can supply values outside the locally generated `createId` alphabet. Recovery previously interpolated that ID directly into `<recovery-root>/<documentId>.jsonl`. A deterministic temporary-root regression assigns `../escaped`, pre-creates an outside sentinel, and traverses production compact, append, workspace-index, recover, and discard. The sentinel remains byte-exact; the only document file is now `document-<sha256(documentId)>.jsonl`; the path-like ID and its immediate rename recover at revision 1 with the exact active identity; discard removes only the hashed journal. A second pair with `Imported-Case-Identity` and `imported-case-identity` produces two distinct hashed files and recovers both, avoiding case-fold collisions on Windows/macOS. Safe direct-child legacy journals remain readable and accept the next transaction; successful compaction writes the hashed snapshot and only then removes the legacy file.

Recovery records now have one exact snapshot envelope followed by a contiguous same-document canonical transaction sequence. A real compact plus valid rename followed by an unterminated JSON fragment returns the renamed revision-1 document. Appending a syntactically valid rename after that fragment does not cross the gap: the same revision-1 prefix returns, because an omitted commit could invalidate every later operation. A separate invalid nested sprite transaction likewise leaves the last snapshot available. Direct malformed, cross-document, and schema-shaped non-JSON appends reject before changing the good journal. The workspace index is used only when it has the exact version-1 key set, unique nonempty IDs, and an active ID included in that order; active-outside-order, duplicate-ID, and extra-field variants fall back to both valid journals with no inferred active tab.

The production `DocumentService.open` path now queues a migrated snapshot before returning an opened native document. A deterministic source archive is opened, renamed immediately without waiting for the 60-second timer, flushed, and recovered by a fresh service with exact ID, source path, dirty state, name, and revision 1. Hashing rather than sanitizing IDs was chosen because opaque case-distinct identity must survive; skipping later records rather than repairing around a gap was chosen because transaction semantics are ordered. No journal byte/count ceiling was added because no established policy permits dropping an otherwise valid high-volume recovery session. Focused journal/service coverage passes 2 files / 23 tests; the broader journal/service/history/trace/transaction/batch/generation/playback/MCP selection passes 9 files / 91 tests. The complete pinned Node-24 `verify` gate passes portability for 369 tracked plus 23 prospective paths, TypeScript, full ESLint, and 122 Vitest files / 734 tests. The unchanged performance gate passes 1/1 at 758.94 MiB RSS growth with every timing under its budget; `/private/tmp/aidraw-recovery-performance-gate.json` is not retained release evidence. This does not claim filesystem fsync/power-loss durability, a resource ceiling, packaged crash behavior, broader-platform acceptance, Level 3, or RC readiness.

Headless native manifest/document and content-addressed-asset integrity evidence (2026-08-12): one writer-produced schema-2 archive opens normally, and its unchanged expanded entries are re-zipped into eight valid-CRC twins that each alter only one manifest summary field: schema, document identity, kind, name, revision, created/updated timestamp, or generic asset count. Every twin now rejects on the named inconsistency before document migration can return canonical content. Seven shape/type/authority variants plus invalid JSON prove malformed objects, foreign format/saver identity, future schema, unsafe revision, and negative asset counts fail closed. A separate writer-produced archive externalizes one fixed eight-byte asset and reopens with exact bytes and no warnings. Re-saving that destination from a live clone whose supplied bytes contradict the unchanged hash rejects before replacement and leaves the destination byte-identical. Valid-CRC variants then change only its entry bytes, declared length, inline/missing layout, or SHA metadata: hash/length mismatches retain metadata but omit data with the exact corrupt warning, an inline copy cannot bypass a missing content-addressed entry, and invalid metadata rejects. Warning-based omission was chosen over whole-document rejection for missing/corrupt payloads to preserve the established partial-recovery contract without exposing unverified artwork; malformed metadata and contradictory identity remain fatal. The matched schema-1 fixture still migrates to schema 2, and checkpoint isolation remains unchanged. Focused persistence/migration/schema coverage passes 3 files / 36 tests; the complete pinned Node-24 gate passes portability over 369 tracked plus 18 untracked paths, clean TypeScript and ESLint, and 120 files / 718 tests. Performance coverage passes 1 file / 1 test. This proves only writer-defined native manifest/asset shape and exact manifest/document plus embedded-payload reconciliation; it does not claim arbitrary corruption recovery, image-decoder validity, broader document-schema validation, packaged/native UI behavior, Level 3, or RC readiness.

Headless durable-trace ingestion and replay-input integrity evidence (2026-08-12): native/local trace readers previously accepted any truthy transaction object after checking only version, outer document ID and a few primitive fields, so cross-document, invalid-operation, oversized, and extra-envelope records could enter the durable store and later reach inspection/replay. One writer-produced archive now retains its ordinary valid trace plus a transaction whose parsed JSON is exactly the established 2 MiB limit. A valid-CRC twin adds 10,000 tiny `{}` lines, an extra-envelope record, a cross-document transaction, an invalid rename operation, and a 2 MiB-plus-one transaction. Native open returns only the two exact valid records in order, preserves the canonical document, and emits one exact `10,004 malformed transaction trace entries were ignored.` warning. The shared scanner proves CRLF/final-line behavior, skips sub-minimum line floods without per-fragment JSON parsing, bounds each envelope, and a deterministic mutation family rejects missing/extra fields, future versions, foreign identities, negative/fractional/unsafe revisions, noncanonical timestamps, unknown outcomes, missing transactions, 257 operations, invalid actors, and invalid canonical operations. A malformed supplied trace also fails native save before replacement and leaves the prior archive byte-identical. Separately, a local trace file containing one valid, one schema-invalid, and one tiny record exposes only the valid record through list/find; import ignores an invalid record and existing duplicate, appends the one new valid record in a single batch, and preserves exact order. The 2 MiB constant is now shared with `DocumentService`, so admission and replayable trace validation cannot drift. A schema-shaped rename carrying one BigInt extra previously escaped as a rejected promise during size serialization; it now returns exact `conflict / Transaction must be JSON-serializable.` with document name, revision and dirty state unchanged. No count truncation was introduced; malformed native records remain auxiliary and cannot prevent otherwise valid artwork from opening. Focused persistence/trace/store/service/playback/transaction/MCP/schema coverage passes 8 files / 93 tests; the complete pinned Node-24 gate passes portability over 369 tracked plus 21 untracked paths, clean TypeScript and ESLint, and 121 files / 723 tests. Performance coverage passes 1 file / 1 test. This is headless trace-ingestion/replay-input integrity within the existing 64 MiB native metadata boundary, not packaged renderer replay, hostile profile-file sizing, broader-platform, Level 3, or RC evidence; FND-08 stays Verified and QA-08 stays Working.

Headless AGT-09 stale-revision contract evidence (2026-08-07): `canvas_apply` output discovery explicitly describes the optional entity-level conflict envelope—canonical entity ID, supplied revision, actual revision and retryability—in addition to its existing canonical `next` step. Interchange sample:

## Level 3 — Release exhaustive

Automated command:

```powershell
node scripts/npm-node24.mjs run test:level3:auto
```

Level 3 is the complete release-candidate gate. It includes Levels 1 and 2 plus:

### Build and clean-machine evidence

- run from a committed, reviewable baseline with a clean dependency install;
- verify installer and portable ZIP on a clean supported Windows 11 x64 environment;
- verify install, launch, attach/headless lifecycle, clean uninstall behavior, portable behavior, SHA-256 checksums, dependency/license report, and documented unsigned-build warning;
- record all artifact hashes and build environment versions.

### Exhaustive MCP contract

- initialize instructions, tools-only cold discovery, progressive help/guide retrieval, strict conditional action schemas, structured output schemas/results, safe next-step guidance, all resources, subscriptions, notifications, tool actions, annotations, malformed payloads, 2 MiB/256-operation limits, four queues, four playback lanes, one-million-sample budget, 32 clients, fairness, cancellation, locks, retries, approvals, timeouts, privacy redaction, and actor-isolated history;
- headless multi-hour-style sequence with checkpoint observation and restart/recovery;
- permission tests for exact paths, trusted folders, reparse/symlink/case aliases, companion exports, overwrites, and unsaved close;
- attribution/provenance and referential-integrity checks represented by SEC-01–05.

### Exhaustive native UI through Computer Use

- enumerate and exercise every visible native menu item, shortcut, dialog, toolbar tool, panel tab, inspector control, timeline control, layer/asset action, status control, approval state, empty/error/loading state, and document type;
- test supported minimum/typical/large window sizes, 100% and 200% display scaling where available, keyboard-only navigation, reduced motion/high contrast where safely testable, focus restoration, modal focus, and readable actor labels;
- perform complete illustration, animated-sprite, tileset/Wang terrain, finite map, infinite map, isometric map, generation-with-mocks, import, export, save, reopen, trace replay, and recovery scenarios;
- visually compare before/after state and cross-check every persisted UI mutation through MCP or reopened native state.

### Interchange and rendering corpus

- run maintained external fixtures for SVG, PDF, PSD, PNG/JPEG/WebP, GIF/APNG, sprite sheets, TMJ/TMX/TSJ/TSX, including supported and deliberately unsupported features;
- compare maintained visual goldens for illustration compositing, brushes, masks, blends, filters, pixel frames, animations, orthogonal maps, and isometric maps;
- require explicit, accurate fallback reports for every intentional fidelity loss.

### Resilience, security, and performance

- malformed/corrupt/truncated/oversized inputs, ZIP bombs/decompression limits, invalid chunks, cyclic references, huge dimensions, interrupted saves, renderer failure/reload, provider failures, and process crashes;
- mocked OpenAI, Stability, and ComfyUI success/failure/cancel/rate-limit/moderation/timeout/ambiguous cases with no paid network call;
- measured pointer latency/frame pacing, 5,000 vectors, four 4K paint layers, large visible tilemap, save/export duration, memory ceiling, and queued-agent isolation against documented budgets.

Level 3 release criteria:

- automated release command passes;
- every mandatory Computer Use and MCP scenario has evidence;
- no open Blocker or P0 defect;
- P1 exceptions are explicitly accepted and linked to tracker IDs;
- release artifacts, report, warnings, and known limitations agree with the README and feature tracker.

### Single-use package-generation roots

Every supported `electron-forge package` or ordinary `electron-forge make` path now reserves its complete output root before Packager reaches its built-in `overwrite: true` removal. The reservation is an atomic directory create followed by `.aidraw-package-generation.json`, a deterministic marker containing only policy version, package name/version, target platform/architecture, and the workspace-relative output name. It contains no timestamp, PID, credential, profile, or user content. A pre-existing directory, file, symlink, concurrent reservation, or prior failed attempt fails closed; `scripts/prepare-make-output.mjs` no longer removes `out/make` or generated manifests. A failed attempt consumes its root. Select a new root instead of silently deleting or reusing it.

The default `out` remains the normal first-generation root in a clean checkout. Additional local or retained subjects must set `AIDRAW_FORGE_OUT_DIR` to a fresh strict workspace child whose real parent already exists. Forge, package verification, checksums, default license output, provenance, and the packaged-E2E default all resolve that same generation; `AIDRAW_E2E_OUT_DIR` remains the higher-priority exact-package selector. Package verification rejects a missing, symlinked, malformed, wrong-version, wrong-target, or wrong-path marker before reading package bytes. This intentionally makes retained pre-policy roots without a marker historical evidence rather than current-verifier inputs: preserve them unchanged and never retrofit a marker merely to pass verification. Provenance validates the marker and records its stable policy/target fields without treating the chosen local output pathname as a reproducibility difference.

The ordinary full release path is `npm run make` from a fresh root; it packages once and then runs makers. `npm run package` is the package-only alternative and must not be followed by an ordinary same-root `npm run make`, because that would ask Forge to replace the package generation. CI/tag jobs remain usable because each starts in a fresh checkout. The policy is filesystem-only: it does not enumerate, attach to, signal, quit, restart, or diagnose a process, and it does not define an installer/updater migration contract. Its evidence is deterministic source/headless coverage of the overwrite ordering, atomic race, first generation, existing-generation refusal with byte preservation, explicit distinct prepared roots, marker verification, root containment, and absence of deletion/process authority—not a native package or customer-update acceptance.

### Release provenance and reproducibility evidence

`npm run provenance` is the final source-focused step of `release:current` and every native tagged build. It requires Node 24, an exact npm version, a supported native host/architecture label, a clean 40-character Git commit, matching root package/lock versions, exact declared build-tool versions, a matching single-use package-generation marker, nonempty maker output, and a checksum manifest whose complete path/hash set agrees with the actual maker files. Windows also requires both generated third-party license reports. It rejects symlink output and writes a release-provenance **schema 2** manifest at `<selected-generation>/RELEASE_PROVENANCE-<platform>.json` with exclusive-create semantics so an earlier subject cannot be silently overwritten. Schema 2 makes `packageGeneration` part of source identity. Retained pre-policy schema-1 manifests remain historical evidence and deliberately compare as incompatible; they are not migrated or treated as equivalent to schema 2. The native release workflow retains each manifest with its artifacts.

Compare two independently captured manifests for the same platform with:

```powershell
node scripts/npm-node24.mjs run reproducibility:compare -- --left=<first-provenance.json> --right=<second-provenance.json> --output=<comparison.json>
```

The comparison exits nonzero unless both inputs are release-provenance schema 2 and candidate commit/version, package-generation policy/target, package/lock identity, Node/npm/toolchain, checksum manifest, license reports, and every nonempty maker artifact path/size/SHA-256 match exactly. Capture timestamps and OS-release strings are retained as diagnostics but do not turn byte-identical artifacts into a failure. The separate reproducibility comparison-report contract remains **schema 1**; it uses exclusive-create semantics and records both input-manifest hashes plus exact field differences. It never normalizes or upgrades an input manifest, ignores an artifact mismatch, or accepts a documented exception automatically.

Stable-v1 `rc:verify -- --stable-v1` now requires repository-contained, fully hashed PASS reports for `windows-x64`, `macos-arm64`, and `linux-x64`, each bound to the current candidate with matching source/toolchain/artifact flags and zero differences. This implementation has deterministic synthetic coverage only: no native rebuild pair or comparison report was produced, so REL-07 remains Partial and no reproducibility claim is earned.

### Machine-verifiable RC preflight

The Level 3 run remains the release-candidate gate; the stable-v1 checklist is a stricter, separate gate. A complete Level 3 certificate does not by itself change unfinished feature-tracker work to Verified or prove stable-v1 readiness.

Run the current-state audit at any time under pinned Node 24:

```powershell
node scripts/npm-node24.mjs run rc:audit
```

`rc:audit` always exits zero after a successful inspection and prints both Level 3 RC and stable-v1 status plus deterministic ranked gaps. It is planning output, never certification. The current audit is intentionally blocked: no fresh Level 3 manifest exists, 25 P0 tracker items are not Verified, no stable-v1 native/signing/reproducibility/prerequisite evidence is supplied, 57 selected-v1 P1 items are neither Verified nor Deferred, and the owned source checkpoint is not yet a committed clean candidate.

Headless FND-09 utility-result resource checkpoint (2026-08-12): the main process now validates one coherent post-IPC policy across export, import, observation, generation, progress, and terminal-error results. Export accepts only the requested format's exact primary MIME/extension and expected companion form; ordinary formats cannot acquire companion files, sprite-sheet requires its single JSON companion, and Tiled requires exactly the request-derived PNG leaf names. The result may contain at most 4,097 total members and 512 MiB of aggregate decoded data before any permissive base64 decode. Import meters the documents' exact JSON UTF-8 representation without creating a whole serialized copy, enforcing 512 MiB, 16,777,216 nodes, and 128 levels before migration. Reports/warnings share a 4,096-entry, 64-KiB-per-string, 1-MiB-serialized ceiling. Generic observation results are bounded by the existing 4 MiB PNG's maximum base64 plus 64 KiB; generation provider metadata is JSON-safe within 64 KiB / 4,096 nodes / 32 levels; progress messages are 4 KiB; and error codes/messages are 256 bytes / 16 KiB. Malformed terminal output retires the worker and queued work recovers fresh, while oversized progress remains invisible without terminating a potentially paid task. The worker applies the same progress predicate before posting and truncates honest error messages on a Unicode boundary.

The focused utility/export/Tiled/provider selection passes 20 files / 170 tests under pinned Node 24.14.0. Exact-meter tests compare escaping, Unicode, lone-surrogate, sparse-array, shared-reference, byte, node, depth, cycle, accessor, and non-JSON behavior against native JSON semantics; the 512 MiB aggregate boundary is tested arithmetically without allocating a ceiling-sized fixture. Supervisor tests prove over-budget reports, warnings, observation errors, metadata, progress, error envelopes, and member counts either recover through a fresh worker or remain safely advisory. A valid unexpected PNG companion is rejected before file callers can observe it, while the production sprite-sheet shape still round-trips. The complete safe `verify` gate passes portability for 369 tracked plus ten prospective paths, TypeScript, full ESLint, and 115 Vitest files / 638 tests. The unchanged non-GUI performance gate passes 1/1 on local Apple Silicon at 734.41 MiB RSS growth with every timing below its existing budget; its report was redirected to a temporary path and does not exercise the new ceiling-sized utility results.

The 512 MiB decoded-export/import-JSON ceilings align with the existing native expanded-container safety scale while leaving room for the established 64-megapixel export surface; they do not claim that base64 IPC itself is 512 MiB. Trusting the child to self-report size was rejected because the main process owns the boundary, and whole-result `JSON.stringify` metering was rejected because it creates another ceiling-sized allocation. Terminating generation for oversized progress was rejected because progress is advisory and a provider request may already be billable. These checks run after Electron structured-clone delivery, so they are not a pre-deserialization, exact-RSS, real-provider, packaged/native, broader-platform, release-pipeline, or release-candidate certificate. FND-09 remains Working pending those independent gaps.
