Headless GEN-05 credential-lifecycle checkpoint (2026-08-12): OpenAI and Stability mutations now share one serialized queue so concurrent read/modify/write cannot drop the other provider. A nonempty rotation is limited to 16 KiB of UTF-8, encrypted first, written to a uniquely created temporary requested with mode `0600` on POSIX, flushed, closed, and atomically replaced; encryption, validation, or replacement failure leaves the prior file byte-exact and cleans the temporary. Removing one provider preserves the other, while removing the last deletes the credential file. Removal deliberately does not require decryption or an available secure backend, allowing a user to discard stored ciphertext after DPAPI/Keychain/libsecret becomes unavailable. The UI exposes Replace and a two-click Remove action; backend-unavailable status explains whether encrypted material remains, disables setting/replacing, and keeps removal available.

The renderer never reads a hosted key back. Its frozen provider surface remains exactly `setProviderCredential` and `getProviderStatus`; status contains configured/stored/availability flags plus an OS-owned failure reason, never plaintext or ciphertext. Direct store regressions cover concurrent providers, rotation, both removal shapes, encrypted-at-rest output, encryption/replacement/size failure preservation, temporary cleanup, and unavailable Windows DPAPI without decrypt/encrypt calls. The preload regression proves write/status only. The focused provider/platform/preload/renderer-security/package-security/generation selection passes 7 files / 60 tests under pinned Node 24.14.0; the complete safe gate passes portability for 369 tracked plus 11 prospective paths, TypeScript, full ESLint, and 116 Vitest files / 642 tests. The unchanged non-GUI performance gate passes 1/1 on local Apple Silicon at 753.19 MiB RSS growth with every timing below its established budget; its report was redirected to `/private/tmp/aidraw-gen05-performance-gate.json` and is not retained release evidence.

Direct overwrite was rejected because interruption could corrupt the only credential file; independent unqueued updates were rejected because they could lose the other provider; secret/ciphertext retrieval was rejected because renderer status does not need it; and requiring backend access for removal was rejected because loss of DPAPI/Keychain/libsecret must not trap encrypted material. The existing setter carries empty-string removal to avoid expanding the frozen privileged bridge. HTML `maxLength` is only an early code-unit UX bound; the main-process UTF-8 byte limit is authoritative. Corrupt-file recovery still fails closed to an empty logical store rather than guessing or exposing data. This checkpoint does not exercise real DPAPI/Keychain/libsecret, a live provider or credential, packaged UI interaction, cross-build migration, broader platforms, Level 3, or an RC.

Headless QA-03/IO-01 JPEG-orientation checkpoint (2026-08-12): a locally generated 948-byte baseline JPEG has four solid quadrants and one fixed EXIF APP1/IFD0 orientation-6 entry. Its canonical base64 fixture decodes to SHA-256 `127b13e4ae8a15d43738a0c221d364b019bfb548b66f01c7cdb1e40bc599a3a6`. The header remains 24×16 encoded pixels while the pinned decoder applies a 16×24 display with exact center samples blue `[49,87,212,255]`, red `[239,48,56,255]`, yellow `[242,213,60,255]`, and green `[39,179,92,255]`. Production utility import now uses that display geometry, retains the original JPEG bytes, emits the exact encoded-to-display warning, passes shared inline-asset admission, survives native save/reopen, and produces a PNG with byte-exact RGBA equality to the decoder reference. Pixel-mode import also becomes 16×24 and maps those quadrants to the expected default palette entries instead of stretching encoded coordinates.

The APP1 parser is bounded by the declared JPEG segment and IFD entry bytes, accepts only an EXIF TIFF header plus orientation tag `0x0112` with SHORT/count one/value 1–8, and supports both big- and little-endian TIFF. A hostile `0xffffffff` IFD offset is ignored without an out-of-bounds read or orientation claim; the ordinary encoded dimensions remain available, and any decoder disagreement still fails closed. Encoded dimensions remain explicit in the header contract; a separate helper derives display dimensions, avoiding a silent semantic change for PNG/GIF/WebP and non-oriented JPEG callers. The focused raster/import/security/persistence/generation selection passes 6 files / 65 tests under pinned Node 24.14.0; the complete safe gate passes portability for 369 tracked plus 14 prospective paths, TypeScript, full ESLint, and 117 Vitest files / 645 tests. The unchanged non-GUI performance gate passes 1/1 on local Apple Silicon at 746.67 MiB RSS growth with every timing under its established budget; its report was redirected to `/private/tmp/aidraw-exif-performance-gate.json` and is not retained release evidence.

Re-encoding the JPEG on import was rejected because it would discard the exact source and introduce another lossy generation. Storing an AIDraw rotation transform was rejected because browser/main decoder orientation could then be applied twice and pixel-mode quantization would still need separate handling. Treating metadata dimensions as decoded dimensions was rejected because callers still need the encoded geometry for validation and diagnostics. This checkpoint does not define arbitrary EXIF preservation/export, ICC/color-management behavior, malformed-EXIF rejection beyond safe ignore, camera/vendor compatibility, sprite-sheet/Tiled coordinate conventions, other orientation-value pixel goldens, packaged/native UI, Level 3, or RC evidence. IO-01 and QA-03 remain Working.

Headless QA-01 core-transaction checkpoint (2026-08-12): the operation corpus is compile-time exhaustive over all 41 `CanvasOperation` kinds. Every scenario validates the forward payload, applies it, validates every generated inverse payload, restores semantic state, validates redo, and restores the forward result. Illustration layers and objects now use complete bounded discriminated schemas instead of kind-only fallback validation. Replacement operations cannot smuggle changes to layer parentage, vector membership, object layer, or group membership; newly added vector/group layers must start empty and use ordered add/move operations, while object-group children must already exist in the same layer, have one parent, and remain acyclic. Checkpoint merge topologically adds masks/group children before dependants. Ordered provenance returns to its original index, deleting the active pixel asset restores active identity, and undoing a first pixel/tile write removes the all-zero sparse chunk instead of retaining dense absence.

Eight fixed seeds per illustration and pixel family each run 48 valid operations, validate the document structure after every forward/undo/redo step, and return first to the initial semantic state and then to the forward state. A separate 32-seed family proves a late failure rolls back the whole multi-operation transaction. These reducer tests use `rebaseTransactionExpectedRevisions` only as a same-lineage primitive; its contract explicitly requires the caller to prove that no foreign overlap occurred.

`DocumentService` now makes that proof with internal logical-target lineage. A history entry unions the forward and inverse targets; whole pixel-asset targets contain nested frames, cels, and tile layers, and object-group relationships carry per-child membership targets. The current actor's top entry may rebind monotonic revision guards. A later foreign overlap permanently blocks crossing that entry, an explicitly unrecorded same-actor mutation creates the same barrier, and any foreign commit blocks a document-revision-guarded entry because that token remains global. Disjoint actor targets stay independently undoable; a new own edit after foreign work can undo back to the foreign state but not through it. A blocked top disables the matching snapshot action and direct invocation returns an attributed conflict. Successful undo/redo commits now enter the same bounded revision change feed as ordinary commits. Blind rebasing was rejected because it could overwrite another actor's work; per-field rebasing of document-wide revision guards was rejected because it would silently broaden the established concurrency contract.

The focused reducer/schema/material/checkpoint/history/service/MCP selection passes 11 files / 155 tests under pinned Node 24.14.0. The complete safe `verify` gate passes portability for 369 tracked plus 18 prospective paths, TypeScript, full ESLint, and 120 Vitest files / 716 tests. The unchanged non-GUI performance gate passes 1/1; no performance report was retained as release evidence. `rc:audit` remains non-certifyingly blocked, now with 25 non-Verified P0 rows after QA-01 satisfies its automated exit criteria. This checkpoint does not persist undo stacks across restart, implement semantic merge/CRDT history, prove packaged/native UI actions, exercise other platforms, create Level 3 evidence, or establish an RC.

After a fresh independent Level 3 run, copy [the evidence template](testing/RC_EVIDENCE_TEMPLATE.json) into that ignored `test-results/luna-high/<run-id>/` root, replace every placeholder with observed evidence, and run:

```powershell
node scripts/npm-node24.mjs run rc:verify -- --evidence=test-results/luna-high/<run-id>/rc-evidence.json
```

Verification fails closed unless the manifest matches the current `package.json` version and 40-character Git HEAD, both the manifest and actual checkout are clean, the independent `gpt-5.6-luna`/`high` Level 3 result and exact `test:level3:auto` exit are PASS/0, mandatory MCP/Computer Use/cross-surface evidence is complete, Blocker/P0 defect arrays are empty, every P1 exception names an existing P1 tracker ID plus its accepter and rationale, and README/tracker/changelog/known-limitations reconciliation is explicit. The report must be a repository-contained regular file with its full SHA-256 and must contain the candidate commit, required Level 3 identity/result markers, evidence sections, coverage exceptions, and final decision.

The manifest also requires native verify/package evidence and repository-contained full-SHA-256 files for `windows-x64:squirrel`, `windows-x64:zip`, `windows-x64:checksums`, `macos-arm64:dmg`, `macos-arm64:zip`, `macos-arm64:checksums`, `linux-x64:deb`, `linux-x64:rpm`, `linux-x64:zip`, `linux-x64:checksums`, `all:licenses-json`, and `all:licenses-markdown`. Windows clean-install lifecycle evidence is part of Level 3. Add `--stable-v1` to require macOS/Linux clean-install evidence, every P0 Verified, every selected-v1 P1 Verified or Deferred, the complete utility/corrupt/provider/performance/accessibility/E2E gate, macOS native package lifecycle, signing/notarization or the explicitly documented unsigned limitation allowed by the checklist, three exact hashed per-platform reproducibility PASS reports, dependency/license review, and repository/publication prerequisites.

This preflight is deliberately separate from `verify`, `release:current`, and `test:level3:auto`: exact reports and artifact hashes exist only after packaging and independent native QA, so gating the build that creates them would be circular. The verifier indexes and cross-checks evidence; it does not launch Electron, generate or accept evidence, auto-edit tracker status, infer stable-v1 scope, sign, tag, publish, or turn an unsigned build into a signed one. A prose-only assertion, a self-authored implementation-task report, auto-promoting P0/P1 rows, and silently blocking ordinary source verification were rejected because each would weaken either evidence independence or the normal development gate.

## Latest headless pixel-tool checkpoint

Headless PIX-04 bounded pixel-tool evidence (2026-08-12): the prior UI flood/wand used an unbounded object-and-string DFS, replacement materialized one point per match, semantic flood rejected any sprite over one million total cells before examining its connected component, parametric ellipse rounding had no exact golden, global pencil deduplication erased later revisits, and `pointercancel` invoked the commit path. A shared core surface now caches decoded 32×32 chunks, preserves sequential revisits while removing doubled orthogonal corners, returns symmetric integer point/line/ellipse outlines, and compacts four-connected or replacement regions directly into ordered row runs. Flood and wand cap changed cells at 1,000,000, allowing a one-pixel island in a 1,001×1,000 sparse sprite; replacement retains the stricter one-million inspected-target ceiling because its whole requested scope must be read. Both reject a 65,537th row run and return no partial result. UI sampling intentionally remains visible-composite while semantic operations intentionally remain addressed-cel, preserving established surface meaning.

Fill and replacement are one-shot previews, so pointer movement cannot append brush samples. `pointercancel` and Escape synchronously clear pixel/tile/map-object drafts and selection offsets, then await and best-effort release each unique acquired lock; they never call finish/apply. Partial fills, a larger budget, duplicated UI/MCP algorithms, and cancel-as-commit were rejected because they would either exceed the canonical operation contract or make user-visible state depend on the event surface. Pure kernels cover exact runs, deterministic reference fields, barriers, 8,192² sparse islands, cell/run overflow, pre-read replacement rejection, staircase revisits, ellipse symmetry/degenerates/8-connectivity, and cached cel reads. Cancellation tests prove synchronous clearing, promise/lock-ID deduplication, and contained acquisition/release failures; renderer source wiring proves shared kernels, compact region commits, one-shot movement, and both cancel events; live MCP proves the former large-sprite false rejection is removed and an over-limit component leaves the document exact. Focused typecheck/ESLint and 4 files / 46 tests pass. The complete safe Node-24 gate passes portability for 369 tracked plus 30 prospective paths, TypeScript, full ESLint, and 125 files / 775 tests. The formal performance gate fills a real one-million-cell indexed cel into 1,000 runs in 172.1 ms under a new 2,000 ms tripwire; all metrics pass at 818.31 MiB RSS growth, and `/private/tmp/aidraw-pixel-tools-performance-gate.json` is temporary rather than retained release evidence. The non-certifying audit remains Level 3 `BLOCKED` 2 and stable v1 `BLOCKED` 5, with 25 unfinished P0, 58 selected-v1 P1 rows unclosed, and 96 dirty paths. This closes PIX-04's automated exit criteria only; packaged pointer behavior, broader native/platform evidence, Level 3, and RC readiness remain unclaimed.

## Latest headless selected-tile animation checkpoint

Headless MAP-03 selected-tile evidence (2026-08-12): each tileset animation remains one canonical ordered list of tile IDs and positive millisecond durations. A native drag handle and keyboard up/down buttons call the same immutable move kernel and submit the resulting tileset through one existing revision-checked `pixel.asset.replace` transaction. Numeric edits are rounded and bounded to the editor's existing tile-ID and 1–60,000 ms ranges. The inspector preview follows Tiled tileset semantics rather than the source sprite's separate frame timeline: it composites the source sprite's first frame, crops only the addressed tile through the map canvas's shared layered pixel compositor, advances by each authored duration, and exposes play/pause. Reduced-motion users start paused. A crop-only compositor was chosen over building a full sprite-sized bitmap because a valid 8,192×8,192 source would make a tiny preview allocate hundreds of megabytes; previewing the sprite timeline was rejected because that would display different semantics from Tiled export.

The maintained hand-authored fixture pairs are documented under `tests/fixtures/tiled/README.md`; they are deterministic Tiled 1.10/1.11.2-shaped inputs rather than proof from a particular Tiled binary. The TMJ/TSJ fixture carries three ordered frames (tile 1 / 120 ms, tile 0 / 80 ms, tile 1 / 200 ms), while TMX/TSX carries two (tile 1 / 90 ms, tile 0 / 60 ms). Each production import → export with ordinary PNG companion → re-import assertion preserves that exact order and timing together with probability, typed properties, and rectangle/polyline collision metadata. Pure tests prove move immutability/index rejection and source-rectangle resolution; a real headless canvas test proves the crop compositor selects only the addressed tile; renderer-source checks bind drag/drop, keyboard controls, duration scheduling, play/pause, and reduced-motion policy. ## Latest headless sparse-isometric depth-order checkpoint

Headless MAP-08 evidence (2026-08-12): tilemap chunks are sparse storage records whose JavaScript insertion order can differ after import, editing, or negative/infinite-map work. Both tile renderers previously traversed `Object.values(layer.chunks)` directly. Within one canonical 32×32 chunk the loop was right-down, but reverse-inserted adjacent chunk bands could composite a visually higher row after the lower row at their overlap. A shared generator now sorts bands by chunk Y and chunks within a band by X, then yields every nonzero cell in complete global rows from left to right. This matches the `renderorder="right-down"` convention AIDraw already writes to Tiled.

The exact visual regression builds two opaque 4×4 source tiles, writes the lower row-32 tile first so chunk `0,1` precedes `0,0`, and overlaps it with the row-31 tile in an isometric map. Two exclusive pixels retain their source colors and the shared overlap pixel equals the lower/front tile. Pure coverage proves band/row/X ordering, input immutability, zero-cell omission, and fail-closed skipping of a decoded payload whose length contradicts chunk geometry. Source coverage binds the same iterator to interactive and headless renderers, while the maintained raw-RGBA rendering golden remains exact.

Only isometric layers use the ordering iterator. Orthogonal maps retain the prior streaming loop. The generator sorts lightweight chunk references and decodes only one Y band at a time; it does not allocate one object per possible map cell at the established 16,777,216-cell import ceiling. ## Latest headless placed-tile animation checkpoint

Headless MAP-03 surface evidence (2026-08-12): per-tile animation metadata, editing, preview, and Tiled round trips were already verified, but a placed map GID still rendered only its base definition. Interactive maps give every animation one session-local epoch, so equal definitions remain phase-aligned across cells and layers. Scheduling targets the nearest authored boundary but is clamped to at least 16 ms; the renderer samples current elapsed time after delayed ticks rather than accumulating timeout drift. A live reduced-motion preference freezes the map at time zero and restarts the session phase when motion is re-enabled. ## Latest headless bounded placed-tile source checkpoint

Headless MAP-03/MAP-07/MAP-08 resource evidence (2026-08-12): both map renderers previously built the nominal full source-sprite bitmap before cropping one placed tile. A valid sparse 8,192×8,192 source therefore created a roughly 256 MiB RGBA intermediate even when the map addressed only 16×16 pixels, and each interactive animation tick rebuilt that full surface. One DOM-neutral compositor now preserves the established visible nested layer order, cumulative opacity, blend modes, linked-cel resolution, frame palette override, and nominal sprite clipping while drawing only an addressed region.

The crop planner computes the smallest integer pixel envelope where the requested source intersects the nominal sprite, then retains the original fractional sampling rectangle inside that bounded canvas. This preserves Canvas2D nearest-neighbor behavior for non-integer authored source offsets and for crops clipped by the right/bottom sprite edge; a fully out-of-bounds crop remains transparent, and signed stored cel chunks outside nominal sprite dimensions do not become visible. Expanding sprite bounds from sparse chunk storage was rejected because it would change the established canvas contract and repeat the unresolved infinite-map origin policy in a different model.

Each render owns a 64 MiB / 1,024-entry least-recently-used crop cache keyed by sprite, source frame, and exact rectangle. Repeated placed cells reuse one flattened crop. Entry count bounds tiny-canvas object overhead, byte count bounds retained pixel surfaces, least-recently-used eviction is deterministic, and native/browser canvases are reset when evicted or when the render ends. A crop larger than the cache byte ceiling remains transient and is reset immediately after its draw; this avoids retaining it but does not claim that an authored 8,192×8,192 tile can render without a correspondingly large temporary surface.

Exact regressions compare fractional bounded/full-source sampling byte-for-byte, keep out-of-nominal stored pixels transparent, exercise deterministic cache refresh/eviction/transient disposal, bind both production renderers to the same planner and cache limits, and address the final 16×16 tile in a sparse 8,192×8,192 source through headless map rendering. ## Latest headless bounded tilemap-raster checkpoint

Headless MAP-07/MAP-08/FND-09 allocation evidence (2026-08-12): `canvas_observe` previously checked a requested crop against its 4,194,304-pixel output budget and then called the full tilemap renderer before cropping. A valid nominal map could therefore allocate an unsafe full native canvas even for a tiny far-edge request. Raster-backed PNG/JPEG/WebP/SVG/PDF export likewise rendered before checking its scaled format limits, and a scale-1 fast path skipped the check entirely.

One nominal-region renderer now shares the established tile source, animation-at-zero, GID transform, nested visibility/opacity, right-down isometric order, projection, and map-object overlay code with full rendering. It culls nonintersecting tile cells before resolving source crops and renders into only the requested surface. Visible Canvas2D object strokes can depend on pixels immediately outside a crop boundary, so ordinary regions use a four-pixel backing margin followed by raw `getImageData`/`putImageData` extraction; the margin contracts deterministically rather than exceeding the 65,535-side or 64-megapixel static-raster ceiling. Exact orthogonal and isometric regressions compare requested tile/object RGBA byte-for-byte with a full-raster crop. This is nominal-region rendering, not stored-chunk bounds expansion.

Observation checks the output side before either source or output canvas allocation. `observation_dimensions_too_large` is a distinct child/main-validated result carrying the 65,535 side limit; `observation_too_large` remains the aggregate pixel-budget result. A million-column map can return its exact 16×16 far-edge tile while its full observation is rejected structurally. The capture releases both source and output native surfaces after encoding. Whole static map rendering and raster-backed exports preflight nominal dimensions and reject above 65,535 per side or 64 megapixels before rendering. Pixel SVG reuses the already rendered bounded PNG instead of rendering a second composite.

Native save deliberately has a different outcome from export: the canonical `.aidraw` document must remain saveable even when an auxiliary whole preview is unsafe. The lazy preview supplier checks the same dimensions and returns no preview, causing the writer's established transparent 1×1 fallback to be archived. Blocking a valid save, silently downsampling an export, or redefining the map bounds from sparse storage were rejected because those choices would respectively risk authored work, change output semantics, or cross the unresolved infinite-map origin policy.

## Latest headless interlaced-APNG checkpoint

Headless PIX-14/IO-07/QA-03/QA-08 evidence (2026-08-12): pixel-mode APNG import already used a bounded custom decoder to recover editable frames, but it rejected every IHDR Adam7 payload before color conversion. Delegating animated import to a second native/general decoder was rejected because the established frame rectangles, timing, disposal/blend behavior, exact source retention, 10,000-frame ceiling, and 256-million-frame-pixel budget already belong to this parser. The fix therefore stays inside that authority and adds no dependency or new resource value.

The decoder first calculates the exact inflated size across the seven Adam7 pass geometries and retains zlib's existing `maxOutputLength` tripwire. Each nonempty pass is unfiltered as its own image, then its samples are placed into the same packed canonical row layout consumed by the existing color conversion. Byte-aligned samples copy complete pixels; one-, two-, and four-bit samples are unpacked and repacked most-significant-bit first. The animated path now also requires valid CRCs on every chunk, one increasing sequence shared by `fcTL` and `fdAT`, agreement between `acTL` frame count and observed controls, and one empty IEND at exact EOF. Interlace methods other than 0/1, illegal PNG color-type/depth pairs, and structural or inflated-row contradictions fail before a document exists; a static PNG without `acTL` continues to the established general raster path. Noninterlaced conversion and frame compositing remain shared and unchanged.

One generated 8×8 8-bit RGBA APNG exercises all seven passes plus a 3×3 second-frame rectangle at offset (2,3). Exact RGBA proves the 50/70 ms frames, blend-source behavior, previous-frame disposal metadata, transparency, and composited pixels before the production importer returns two editable cels and retains byte-exact `image/apng` source data. A separate generated 8×8 one-bit indexed APNG proves packed samples and `tRNS` transparency map to the exact palette pixels. Re-encoding the first fixture's rows in noninterlaced layout while retaining interlace method 1 rejects on the exact inflated-byte mismatch. Five length-preserving or boundary variants independently reject a bad `acTL` CRC, a skipped second-frame sequence, declared three versus observed two frames, missing IEND, and one byte after IEND before canonical import.

## Latest headless APNG color-metadata checkpoint

Headless PIX-14/IO-07/QA-03/QA-08 evidence (2026-08-12): the editable-frame APNG color conversion previously accepted `PLTE`/`tRNS` without reconciling their order, shape, color-type legality, bit-depth range, or indexed sample membership. An undeclared indexed sample could therefore fall through as black with default opacity. The 16-bit grayscale/truecolor path also reduced each channel to its high display byte before comparing that byte with the low byte of the two-byte transparent key, so it did not implement exact encoded-sample equality.

The parser now allows at most one palette before transparency/image data and at most one transparency chunk after an indexed palette and before image data. Before inflation it validates palette triplet length and 256-entry ceiling, indexed palette capacity for the declared depth, exact grayscale/truecolor transparency payload widths and sample ranges, indexed transparency length, and the prohibition on transparency for alpha-bearing color types. Indexed pixels must address a real palette entry. At depth 16, both encoded bytes of every grayscale or truecolor sample participate in transparent-key equality; only afterward does the established conversion retain the high byte for the canonical 8-bit display color. Invalid metadata fails before document construction instead of synthesizing fallback pixels.

Generated 2×1 grayscale and truecolor APNG controls each contain one exact transparent-key sample and a second sample differing only in one low byte. Their displayed colors are identical, but exact RGBA and editable-cel assertions prove only the first pixel is transparent, timing remains 60 ms, and the original `image/apng` bytes remain embedded. Ten exact malformed variants cover a non-triplet palette, three entries at indexed depth one, indexed transparency longer than its palette, a stored index outside a shortened palette, duplicate palette and transparency chunks, a forbidden grayscale palette, invalid grayscale/truecolor transparency ranges after an 8-bit header mutation, and transparency on RGBA.

The complete safe gate passes portability for **369 tracked + 76 prospective** paths, TypeScript, full ESLint, and **151 files / 879 tests**. ## Latest headless APNG default-image checkpoint

Headless PIX-14/IO-07/QA-03/QA-08 evidence (2026-08-12): an APNG can make the PNG default image its first animation frame by placing `fcTL` before `IDAT`, or keep that fallback outside the animation by placing the first `fcTL` after `IDAT`. The editable-frame parser already happened to discard an uncontrolled default image, but it did not represent these modes explicitly: a later nonconsecutive `IDAT` could be accepted as animation data, `fdAT` could be appended to the included first frame, and the excluded fallback's compressed rows were never validated.

Each parsed frame now records whether its data must come from `IDAT` or `fdAT`. A control before the first image data selects `IDAT`, must cover the complete IHDR canvas at offset zero, accepts a contiguous split stream, and rejects `fdAT`. Image data before the first control is retained only long enough to validate its exact inflated rows and filters under the existing per-frame ceiling; it never enters the transparent animation canvas. That mode lets the first animation control describe an ordinary partial rectangle but requires its compressed bytes through `fdAT`. Any non-`IDAT` chunk closes the PNG image-data sequence, and a new frame control cannot replace one that has no payload.

One generated 2×1 8-bit RGBA APNG splits a blue/green fallback across two consecutive `IDAT` chunks, then supplies a 1×1 pink frame at offset (1,0) through `fdAT`. Exact RGBA and editable-cel assertions prove pixel (0,0) remains transparent rather than inheriting the fallback, pixel (1,0) is the sole painted index, timing is 90 ms, and the exact source bytes remain embedded. Five malformed variants independently reject a five-byte fallback where nine inflated bytes are required, `IDAT` used for a frame after the separate fallback, a partial first frame claiming the included `IDAT`, `fdAT` appended to that included first frame, and an `IDAT` resumed after a `tEXt` separator.

## Latest headless APNG chunk-type checkpoint

Headless PIX-14/IO-07/QA-03/QA-08 evidence (2026-08-12): after `acTL` selected the editable-frame parser, every unrecognized PNG chunk was previously ignored. That is correct only for ancillary chunks. A type whose first letter is uppercase is critical, so decoding its surrounding standard chunks while ignoring its required semantics can produce a plausible but incorrect animation. The parser also did not enforce PNG's four-letter ASCII chunk alphabet or the reserved uppercase third type bit.

One parser-local validator now requires every APNG chunk type byte to be `A`–`Z` or `a`–`z` and rejects a lowercase third byte. After all supported PNG/APNG types have been processed, an unrecognized uppercase-first type rejects before pixel conversion; an unrecognized lowercase-first ancillary type remains ignored. The original source buffer is still embedded rather than reconstructed, so an accepted ancillary chunk survives byte-for-byte. Static PNGs without `acTL` still return before this path and remain under the established general raster decoder.

The positive control inserts private ancillary `vpAg` with three data bytes before `acTL` in the generated 2×1 16-bit truecolor fixture. Exact RGBA remains identical, editable import stays 2×1 at 60 ms, and the embedded source equals the mutated bytes. Three CRC-valid matched variants independently reject unknown critical `ABCD`, reserved-third-bit `abcz`, and non-letter `a1Cd` before document creation.

The complete safe gate passes portability for **369 tracked + 76 prospective** paths, TypeScript, full ESLint, and **151 files / 883 tests**. ## Latest source/headless frame-local animation-palette checkpoint

Source/headless PIX-12/PIX-14/QA-03 evidence (2026-08-12): GIF/APNG import previously composited frames correctly but quantized every result against the default document palette, discarding exact local-table and full-RGBA frame colors even when they fit the canonical indexed model. One deterministic planner now scans each composited frame after the existing alpha threshold. Reference colors retain their established nonzero indexes, novel RGBA colors receive the lowest unused slots in raster-discovery order, and every frame palette keeps identical length, entry IDs, and order so later colors fit the existing `paletteOverrides` contract. Index zero remains transparent, leaving 255 visible colors per frame.

The planner is all-or-fallback: if any frame contains more than 255 visible RGBA colors, it returns no partial plan, every frame follows the established default-palette quantizer, and import reports that fallback while retaining the exact source bytes. Mixing exact and quantized frames was rejected because the fidelity mode would otherwise change silently within one animation. APNG planning reuses the bounded decoder's existing composited frames. GIF uses two disposal-aware compositing passes so palette discovery does not retain an additional full-frame array.

Canvas premultiplication can round RGB channels on partial-alpha readback even when the canonical eight-digit palette color is exact. APNG export therefore reads indexed chunks directly only for exactly one ordinary pixel layer at full opacity and normal blend—the shape produced by animation import—then applies the existing nearest-neighbor scale and encoder. Additional/grouped layers, opacity, or blend continue through the established Canvas renderer. This keeps the precision correction narrow rather than introducing a second general compositor or claiming byte identity for complex edits.

A generated 2×1 two-frame RGBA APNG uses four novel frame-local colors, including alpha 128 and 192. Exact assertions cover document palette colors, aligned second-frame overrides, indexed cel values, embedded source bytes, and decoded APNG re-export bytes. A generated 2×1 two-frame GIF uses different local color tables and proves exact opaque colors, overrides, rendered RGBA, and source retention. A generated one-frame 16×16 APNG has 256 distinct opaque colors and proves the exact warning, unchanged default palette, no overrides, and source retention. Pure planner tests independently cover reference-index reuse, deterministic novel slots, partial alpha, exact pixel changes, and fail-closed overflow.

Focused pinned-Node-24 typecheck/targeted ESLint and **3 files / 29 tests** pass. The complete safe gate passes portability for **369 tracked + 80 prospective** paths, TypeScript, full ESLint, and **153 files / 892 tests**. The formal performance gate passes **1/1** at **859.09 MiB** RSS; 5,000 vectors take **24.24 ms**, editable 4K paint **62.47 ms**, cold/warm materialized paint **192.02 / 165.57 ms**, 65,536 addressed tiles **150.85 ms**, map/sprite sparse regions **1.41 / 0.34 ms**, 100,000-object filtering **39.26 ms**, native save **125.28 ms**, PNG export **42.18 ms**, one-million-sample accounting **1.4 ms**, and flood **171.04 ms**. `/private/tmp/aidraw-frame-local-palette-performance-gate.json` is temporary self-authored diagnostics, not retained release evidence.

## Latest source/headless exact GIF re-export checkpoint

Source/headless PIX-12/PIX-14/QA-03 evidence (2026-08-12): after exact frame-local import, GIF export still rendered each frame to RGBA and requantized it. The existing single-layer indexed resolver now also returns the source index plane plus per-frame RGB palette when every used nonzero color is fully opaque. Integer presentation scaling repeats indexes directly. The GIF encoder uses the first palette as its global table and each subsequent frame palette as a local table, retaining delays, loop, tag sequence, transparent index zero, and established full-frame disposal behavior.

Eligibility is whole-animation and fail-closed. Exactly one ordinary pixel layer at full opacity and normal blend is required, and any used partial-alpha color makes direct GIF output unavailable. If any frame is ineligible, every frame uses the prior Canvas-rendered RGBA quantizer; direct and quantized frames are never mixed. This preserves established behavior for grouped/additional layers, opacity, blend, and GIF's one-bit transparency rather than inventing an alpha-flattening policy. Unused palette colors may be present, but their hidden RGB is not claimed as visual fidelity and the output container is not byte-identical to its source.

The generated 2×1 local-palette GIF from the preceding checkpoint now runs through production import, production GIF export, and production import again. A pure 2×1 canonical sprite proves direct indexes/RGB tables, then proves a used alpha-128 entry and layer opacity 0.5 each reject direct output. Existing 8× nearest-neighbor GIF coverage exercises scaled indexed output.

## Latest source/headless illustration-stroke-style checkpoint

Source/headless ILL-11/QA-02 evidence (2026-08-12): canonical `StrokeStyle` and SVG/PDF export already owned width, opacity, cap, join, and dash, but Canvas consumers applied different subsets. Interactive editable paths ignored opacity/cap/join/dash and did not reject width zero, so the ambient Canvas width could draw an intended zero-width path. Headless paths ignored opacity/cap/join, while headless shapes ignored stroke opacity. Editor pixels, observation/static raster output, and interchange could therefore disagree without any canonical value changing.

One DOM-neutral applicator now assigns the complete stored style to saved Canvas state in both renderers. Stroke opacity multiplies the current context alpha so existing object/group/layer composition remains authoritative; it is not baked into color or canonical state. The Canvas default miter limit is pinned explicitly to 10 in rendering and configured-stroke click geometry. Nonpainted and zero-width strokes issue no draw. Paint resolution remains renderer-local so solid/gradient behavior does not gain another abstraction. Duplicating field subsets was rejected because it caused the drift; adding a user-editable miter field was rejected because no canonical or interchange contract requests one.

## Latest headless regional illustration-observation checkpoint

Source/headless FND-09/FND-10/FND-12/QA-07 evidence (2026-08-12): a targeted illustration observation previously rendered the complete artboard before cropping. On a valid 8,192×8,192 artboard, even a one-pixel far-edge request therefore allocated the full 64-megapixel native surface. Unlike sparse tilemap/cel storage, general Canvas illustration geometry is not phase-invariant when translated to a differently sized backing: curves and dashed or antialiased strokes produced small but real raw-channel differences, while masks, paint, effects, and isolated composites may depend on pixels outside the request.

One pure eligibility contract therefore admits only visible integer-positioned, integer-sized square-corner rectangles with solid/no fill and no visible stroke, inside identity/default structural object groups and layers. Paint layers must contain neither strokes nor persisted tile payloads. The established renderer draws that subset on a four-pixel-expanded in-artboard backing and returns the request through raw `getImageData`/`putImageData`; exact byte parity against a full-artboard crop is required. Exactness over broader optimization was selected; accepting small channel tolerances or silently changing observation pixels was rejected.

## Latest source/headless PSD opacity-contract checkpoint

Source/headless IO-04/QA-03 evidence (2026-08-12): the pinned `ag-psd` contract reads a PSD layer's byte opacity as a normalized `0…1` value and writes by clamping that normalized value before multiplying by 255. AIDraw instead multiplied every export value by 255 before calling the writer and divided every decoded illustration value by 255 again. Every nonzero fractional export therefore became fully opaque, while a decoded 50% layer became about 0.2% in canonical state.

All three PSD writer sites now pass canonical normalized opacity directly: illustration group/vector layers, their AIDraw hidden text companions, and pixel-sprite layers. Illustration PSD import likewise accepts the decoder's normalized value directly, retaining the existing default of one only when opacity is absent. The format byte remains the precision boundary; blend-mode mapping, layer hierarchy, visibility, and raster/composite fallbacks are unchanged.

**Material user-facing decision:** one normalized boundary replaces the broken arithmetic; no value-dependent 0–1/0–255 heuristic is accepted. The pinned decoder cannot produce ambiguous units, and guessing would turn malformed or future values into silent visual changes. Older AIDraw PSD files whose former export already clamped any nonzero fraction to one contain no recoverable intended value, so they import at the full opacity actually stored. At this checkpoint pixel-mode PSD import still created independently quantized sprites; the later hierarchy checkpoint below now applies this same opacity contract within one sprite.

The production AIDraw export→`ag-psd` decode→file import regression proves nested illustration group/vector/hidden-companion opacity **0.8 / 0.6 / 0.4** plus the existing multiply/screen/overlay mappings, text geometry/styles, visibility, and fallback hierarchy. ## Latest source/headless pixel PSD hierarchy-export checkpoint

The temporary native surface is released immediately after copying its image data.

**Material user-facing decision:** PSD receives the current frame's authored layer hierarchy, not a flattened appearance or every animation frame. The report states that later frames are omitted. Pixel-mode PSD import remains its established independent-sprite quantization workflow rather than silently becoming a hierarchy-preserving inverse. Flattening groups, baking composite properties, exporting blank hidden data, duplicating animation frames as an invented layer/frame convention, and changing pixel import in the same slice were rejected.

The production regression creates a locked **0.8 multiply** group containing one locked, hidden **0.25 screen** pixel layer plus a root-ordered empty **0.2 difference** group. ## Latest source/headless PSD expanded-layer-raster preflight checkpoint

Source/headless IO-04/QA-07 evidence (2026-08-12): PSD export retains one full-canvas `ImageData` for every illustration raster fallback or pixel leaf until the writer completes. The established static-raster ceiling bounded each individual surface, but did not bound their aggregate, so a valid canvas with many layers could allocate far beyond the intended expanded-pixel posture before serialization.

The exporter now recursively counts the exact layer rasters its production traversal will create before rendering any of them. Illustration vector/paint leaves and filtered or masked groups each count as one fallback; ordinary groups recurse. Pixel groups recurse and pixel leaves each count once. Structural groups and metadata-only editable-text companions consume no layer-raster entry. Width × height × retained layer-raster count may total at most **64 megapixels**. The separately retained final composite remains subject to the existing per-surface 64-megapixel static-raster limit and is deliberately not folded into the layer aggregate.

**Material user-facing decision:** align export's retained-layer allowance with the existing **64-megapixel PSD expanded-layer import budget**, rather than inventing a format-wide layer-count limit or changing document coordinates/layer semantics. A document above the aggregate limit fails deterministically before export allocation; users must reduce canvas dimensions or raster-bearing layer count. Structural groups and private metadata companions do not consume the allowance because they create no full-canvas image data. Counting the final composite twice, silently flattening layers, or allowing unbounded retained surfaces were rejected.

Pure arithmetic coverage accepts exact boundaries at 8192×8192×1 and 4096×4096×4, then rejects the next layer in each case. Production pixel and illustration controls raise the same explicit 64-megapixel expanded-budget error for two-raster 8192×8192 documents before any large layer canvas can be created. ## Latest source/headless PSD output-structure preflight checkpoint

Source/headless IO-04/QA-07 evidence (2026-08-12): the canonical illustration and pixel schemas deliberately admit far more hierarchy records than PSD import's established 2,048-record and depth-64 safety ceilings. Export previously recursed those canonical trees directly. A broad empty hierarchy could therefore reach the writer with unbounded metadata records, and a deep hierarchy could exceed the safe recursion posture even when it allocated no layer raster and passed the expanded-pixel preflight.

One shared limits module now owns the unchanged import values and the new export contract. Export's cycle-aware preflight mirrors the exact tree it will emit: ordinary groups count as records and recurse; raster leaves count once; an illustration vector with supported visible nonempty text counts its parent record plus one visible-fallback child and every private editable-text companion child. The traversal stops as soon as record **2,049** or depth **65** would be emitted. Text discovery is iterative and bounded by remaining record capacity, so a large companion set cannot first allocate an unbounded result array. Raster-area accounting remains a distinct second check.

**Material user-facing decision:** AIDraw applies the same 2,048-record/depth-64 resource posture in both PSD directions and counts the actual export representation rather than only canonical layers. Oversized documents remain editable and saveable in AIDraw but PSD export fails before writer recursion or layer Canvas work; users must reduce exported hierarchy depth or record count. Silently flattening/dropping layers, treating only raster leaves as structural records, or advertising these AIDraw ceilings as Photoshop format maxima were rejected.

Pure boundary coverage accepts exactly 2,048 records at depth 64 and rejects the next record or depth. Production controls reject 2,049 root empty pixel groups, a cycle-free pixel group chain reaching depth 65, and an illustration whose generated fallback plus **2,046** private text companions would create 2,049 output records. ## Latest source/headless sequential illustration PSD fallback checkpoint

After copying each full-canvas `ImageData`, it resets the temporary native Canvas to 1×1 immediately; the final composite Canvas is released at the same copy boundary before writer entry, so only the bounded pixel copies remain retained for `ag-psd`.

Sequential rendering was chosen over concurrent fallbacks because output order is already canonical and concurrency only multiplies peak work. A document clone, blank hidden fallback, baked root opacity, removal of child/effect semantics, and parallel fallback rendering were rejected.

## Latest source/headless outer SVG viewport checkpoint

Source/headless FND-10/ILL-14/IO-02/QA-03 evidence (2026-08-12): editable SVG import previously treated any outer `viewBox` only as `translate(-minX, -minY)`. When the declared viewport and viewBox differed, AIDraw therefore ignored standard aspect-ratio scaling and alignment. Applying the correct matrix also exposed a renderer defect: every transformed object group was first drawn into an artboard-sized untransformed buffer, so source geometry outside that rectangle was clipped before its group transform could move or scale it into view.

The outermost SVG viewport now resolves one affine matrix from the existing integer artboard and a finite positive four-number `viewBox`. The default is standard `xMidYMid meet`; all valid X/Y alignment tokens accept `meet` or `slice`, and `preserveAspectRatio="none"` permits nonuniform scaling. An invalid value emits `Invalid SVG preserveAspectRatio was reduced to the default xMidYMid meet behavior.` and uses the default instead of guessing. The matrix is represented by an ordinary canonical transform-only group, so stored child coordinates and the document schema do not change.

Interactive and headless Canvas traversal now share two pure predicates. A group whose only special state is an affine transform is applied directly while its children are traversed, allowing geometry outside nominal artboard coordinates to transform into the visible artboard. A group with opacity, non-normal blend, filters, a mask, blur, or shadow still draws to one isolation surface so that composite state applies once. Pre-transform isolation for an effect-bearing transformed group can still clip source overhang; this checkpoint does not silently redefine that effect-envelope policy.

One deterministic generated **200×100** SVG with `viewBox="10 20 100 100"` proves exact canonical transforms and pixels for default horizontal letterboxing, `none` stretching, and `xMidYMax slice`; a malformed alignment proves warned default fallback. Pure tests distinguish identity, transform-only, and each composite/effect reason, and a source contract binds both renderers to the shared branch. Focused pinned-Node-24 typecheck/targeted ESLint and **5 files / 61 tests** pass. The complete safe gate passes portability for **369 tracked + 105 untracked paths**, TypeScript, full ESLint, and **167 files / 958 tests**. The unchanged performance gate passes **1/1** at **870.83 MiB** RSS growth with every metric below its established budget; `/private/tmp/aidraw-svg-viewport-performance-gate.json` is temporary diagnostics, not retained package evidence. The non-certifying audit remains Level 3 `BLOCKED` **2** / stable v1 `BLOCKED` **5**, with **25** unfinished P0, **57** unclosed selected-v1 P1, and **206** dirty paths.

**Material user-facing decision:** standard outer SVG viewport geometry is authoritative, and the importer expresses it through existing canonical coordinates plus one ordinary transform group. Changing stored coordinates, adding offset metadata, flattening every child transform, treating malformed values as `none`, or isolating transform-only groups were rejected. This changes no canonical schema, artboard limit, child coordinate, group effect/composite result, export contract, PDF/PSD/Tiled behavior, or fidelity-report vocabulary. ## Latest source/headless nested SVG viewport checkpoint

A finite positive viewBox whose ratio would create a non-finite matrix also rejects before recursive child construction, preventing unreachable children from leaking into the layer as visible roots; the same outer-root condition rejects before document construction. Source/headless PIX-05/MAP-07/MAP-08/FND-10/FND-12/QA-07 evidence (2026-08-12): the interactive transparency checker previously looped over the complete scaled sprite/map surface and inherited a 24-pixel shadow for its base rectangle plus every dark square. A large canvas viewed at high zoom could therefore submit offscreen checker cells and thousands of separate blurred primitives. Freeform grid lasso separately appended every sampled cell and rerasterized its complete candidate rectangle on every pointer move; the existing one-million-cell check could throw inside that live state update, and path length/boundary work remained unbounded.

One DOM-neutral checker planner now intersects the scaled surface with the local viewport for globally anchored checker indexes and with a fixed 64-pixel-expanded viewport for the existing 24-pixel blur/8-pixel downward offset. The renderer fills and shadows that one bounded source rectangle, clears shadow state, clips to the exact nominal surface edge, and submits all visible dark squares in one path. A nearby fully offscreen surface may retain only its bounded shadow; a farther surface submits nothing.

Grid lasso now stores its outline in a synchronous ref and removes a middle sample only when exact integer cross/dot tests prove that the two segments are collinear and continue in the same direction. It shows that reduced exact outline while dragging and rasterizes cells once on pointer release. Even-odd fill scans along the shorter bounding-box axis, preserving the prior point-membership result while limiting edge checks. Unsafe coordinates, more than **4,096** retained vertices, more than **1,000,000** candidate cells, or more than **4,000,000** Bresenham boundary steps fail closed; the gesture is cancelled or release reports the exact error, and the prior selection is not replaced, cleared, or approximated.

Pure coverage proves the far-edge **524,288×524,288** scaled checker keeps a **1,280×720** visible region, **1,408×848** shadow source, and exactly **28,800** dark cells from **57,600** candidates; it records one shadowed fill, transparent shadow state for the checker path, global parity, clipped edge cells, near-offscreen shadow-only work, no far-offscreen work, and invalid-geometry rejection. Lasso coverage proves **10,001** horizontal plus 20 vertical samples reduce to three exact endpoints, the vertex/area/boundary/safe-coordinate failures occur before raster work, and named horizontal/vertical/concave/negative/self-crossing plus **48** deterministic small polygon controls match the former per-point even-odd reference exactly. Source wiring proves the live renderer uses the bounded checker, validates the full work envelope during sampling, and performs exact rasterization only on release rather than during pointer move.

## Latest source/headless PDF page-resource checkpoint

A primary load/page/viewport/render/raster/text error wins over page-cleanup or task-destroy failure; if import otherwise succeeds, either cleanup failure remains fail-closed.

Direct coverage proves a 4×3 page Canvas resets after successful PNG encoding and an injected render failure, and invalid dimensions reject before Canvas creation. A generated three-page production PDF returns three documents while its observed lifecycle is exactly render 1 / cleanup 1 / render 2 / cleanup 2 / render 3 / cleanup 3 / destroy. Existing malformed-filter render failure with injected cleanup failure retains the exact primary pdf.js `RangeError`; cleanup-only and destroy-only cases reject independently. The page-count, per-page, and five-page aggregate preflight cases respectively clean zero, one, and five acquired proxies while rendering none.

**Material user-facing decision:** aggregate geometry preflight remains more important than starting the first page early, because unsafe later pages must reject before any render. Once accepted, native page resources are released sequentially without changing output. Cleanup failure stays visible rather than being ignored, but it never replaces a more useful primary import error. ## Latest source/headless fractional-opacity exact normal-composite checkpoint

Source/headless PIX-12/PIX-14/IO-01/IO-07/QA-03 evidence (2026-08-12): layer 73 avoided premultiplied Canvas readback only when every normal pixel leaf had full effective opacity. The existing renderer already defines fractional normal semantics unambiguously: visible group/layer opacities multiply along the leaf path, Canvas floors that 0–1 product to an alpha byte, palette alpha multiplies that byte with nearest-integer rounding, and layers compose source-over in canonical order. Sending this supported subset through Canvas needlessly rounded stored partial-alpha RGB.

The exact normal resolver now accepts finite path opacity from zero through one. A single leaf keeps its authored RGB and applies only the renderer-equivalent alpha byte. Multiple leaves apply that same alpha then use the established deterministic unpremultiplied source-over. Fully transparent nonzero colors in a multi-layer frame still decline because hidden-RGB ownership remains ambiguous; every non-normal blend still uses the renderer. GIF now guards effective opacity separately before asking for exact normal-composite indexes, so fractional opacity retains its established whole-animation quantizer even when final alpha happens to be binary.

Pure coverage proves `#4080c080` at leaf opacity 0.5 becomes exact `[64,128,192,64]`, a half-opacity alpha-128 blue over alpha-128 red becomes `[153,0,102,160]`, a half-opacity parent group applies to both leaves, non-normal blends still decline, and exact GIF declines fractional effective opacity. A two-frame production fixture decodes PNG, APNG, and sprite-sheet output to exact `[27,55,228,112,64,128,192,64]` per frame while retaining existing timing and sheet layout. Focused pinned-Node-24 typecheck/targeted ESLint and **3 files / 47 tests** pass. The complete safe gate passes portability for **369 tracked + 107 prospective paths**, TypeScript, full ESLint, and **168 files / 998 tests**. The unchanged formal performance gate passes **1/1** at **876.16 MiB** RSS growth with every established timing below budget; its existing one-million-pixel exact normal composite takes **25.31 ms** under **500 ms**, but it adds no separate fractional-opacity timing or exact allocator-peak assertion. `/private/tmp/aidraw-fractional-opacity-exact-normal-performance-report.json` is temporary diagnostics, not retained package evidence. The non-certifying audit remains Level 3 `BLOCKED` **2** / stable v1 `BLOCKED` **5**, with **25** unfinished P0, **57** unclosed selected-v1 P1, and **213** dirty paths.

**Material user-facing decision:** fractional opacity adopts the pinned renderer's existing byte-rounding contract while preserving source RGB; no new opacity or blend semantics are invented. Requiring full opacity for PNG/APNG/sheet exactness was rejected because it preserved avoidable color drift. Reusing this result for GIF was rejected because GIF's partial/fractional alpha policy is a separate format decision, so its prior eligibility/fallback remains exact. This changes no schema, canonical layer/pixel/opacity values, frame/tag/timing, sheet geometry/metadata, alpha threshold, encoder/profile policy, non-normal blend, GIF, JPEG/WebP/PDF, illustration/tilemap, external-producer, packaged/native, Level 3, RC, or stable-v1 claim; all rows remain Working.

Source/headless FND-09/IO-06/QA-08 evidence (2026-08-13): clipboard copy previously rendered directly in the main process, pixel bitmap paste quantized there through Electron-native decoding, and the private HTML fragment was an unbounded regex/base64 decode with no byte identity. Only HTML without the marker may use a bitmap. Its system-native geometry is checked before PNG encoding; canonical admission then requires supported PNG bytes/header, SHA/length, matching decoded geometry, at most 8,192 pixels per side / 16,777,216 pixels, and the existing 1,500,000-byte editable-asset limit before base64 construction.

Copy asks the existing supervised raster export lane for PNG bytes. Pixel-mode bitmap paste asks the supervised quantization lane for canonical cel changes. Illustration bitmap paste retains the validated PNG as its ordinary image asset. Electron `nativeImage` remains necessarily confined to reading and writing the OS clipboard, not rendering or quantization authority. Source/headless FND-09/QA-08 evidence (2026-08-13): the renderer-facing checkpoint comparison cloned a current document and stored checkpoint, then called `renderDocument` for both concurrently in the Electron browser process. ## Previous source/headless graceful-shutdown integrity checkpoint

Source/headless FND-07/QA-08 evidence (2026-08-13): the explicit **Quit AIDraw Engine** path awaited `EngineRuntime.stop()`, but the ordinary Electron `before-quit` listener only started `void engineRuntime?.stop()` and let process exit continue. This contradicted the established graceful-shutdown contract: MCP/session closure, utility cancellation, final per-document/workspace recovery compaction, and pending replacements could still be running when the app terminated. `EngineRuntime.stop()` also set `started = false` before any work, so a failed final compaction made the next stop return as if shutdown had succeeded.

One DOM/Electron-independent coordinator now owns graceful quit. The first incomplete `before-quit` synchronously calls `preventDefault()`, repeated quit events share one stop, and explicit quit uses the same request. Only successful stop marks completion and calls `app.quit()`; its resulting `before-quit` passes through without prevention. Failure keeps the app alive, clears the request for retry, and reports one stderr diagnostic for a coalesced OS request. CLI/no-runtime exits explicitly mark completion and retain their existing direct exit behavior.

`EngineRuntime.stop()` now shares one in-flight promise across concurrent callers and marks `started = false` only after MCP stop, both utility stops, final recovery compaction, and status publication succeed. A failure clears only the in-flight promise and leaves the runtime eligible to retry the complete idempotent shutdown sequence. It does not restart workers or accept new work during the failed-stop interval.

Deterministic coordinator coverage blocks engine stop, sends two OS events plus another quit request, proves both events prevent exit, one stop runs, and no quit occurs until release; the reissued event then passes through. Separate failure tests prove cancelled quit, no app quit, retry success, and one report across repeated OS events. Production source binds both explicit and `before-quit` paths and excludes the former unobserved stop. A runtime barrier proves two callers share one MCP stop, both receive injected final-recovery failure, `started` stays true, the next stop reruns and succeeds, and later stop is a no-op.

Focused pinned-Node-24 typecheck, targeted ESLint, and **4 files / 39 tests** pass. The complete safe gate passes portability for **369 tracked + 141 prospective paths**, TypeScript, full ESLint, and **184 files / 1,084 tests**. The formal performance gate passes **1/1** at **878.36 MiB** RSS growth, with native save **127.07 ms** and PNG export **41.64 ms**, all below established budgets; it adds no shutdown-specific timing or power-loss metric. Ignored `test-results/performance-gate.json` remains local diagnostics. The non-certifying audit remains Level 3 `BLOCKED` **2** / stable v1 `BLOCKED` **5**, with **25** unfinished P0, **57** unclosed selected-v1 P1, and **268** dirty paths (**127 modified + 141 untracked**).

**Material user-facing decision:** an ordinary quit request is not successful until the documented engine stop and final recovery complete. If that work fails, AIDraw remains alive, emits a diagnostic, and lets the next quit retry; silently exiting after failed compaction or treating a prior partial stop as complete was rejected. Existing explicit confirmation is unchanged. No automatic timeout/force-quit dialog was invented because abandoning recovery needs a separately reviewed UX/data-loss contract. This adds no crash/power-loss/directory-fsync guarantee, packaged OS-quit proof, Level 3, RC, or stable-v1 readiness. FND-07 remains Verified through the new automated acceptance; QA-08 remains Working.

All supported start-at-login mutations now share one rejection-recovering process queue. Windows and macOS retain their exact native calls. Disablement unlinks the named entry in the same queue. The returned status is sampled after that request's mutation, and a rejected request does not poison later work.

It does not roll back a valid external config or falsely return `manual`. Isolated packaged validation retains its exact no-startup-change message and does not invoke the follow-up.

A separate injected first replacement failure proves exact prior bytes, cleanup, and successful queued retry. Completion tests prove truthful configured status/paths/manual-start guidance after a startup exception and the isolated no-call boundary. Existing writer tests still cover Codex TOML, Claude Code, current/obsolete OpenCode JSONC, Antigravity, generic setup, and malformed-input refusal.

## Latest source/headless advisory native-trace import checkpoint

Source/headless FND-06/FND-07/FND-08/QA-08 evidence (2026-08-13): native Open installed a validated document, checkpoint state, and queued recovery compaction before awaiting its separate trace-store import. If that auxiliary append rejected, the outer file-level catch nevertheless omitted the path from `opened`, even though the artwork remained installed and recoverable. The result therefore described the opposite of canonical state and also lost native parsing warnings accumulated before trace import.

Trace import now has its own advisory error boundary. Native parsing warnings are retained in their original order; an import rejection emits the existing `trace-error` signal, adds one path-scoped warning, and then reports the valid artwork as opened and active. This follows the established trace policy: malformed archive records are omitted with warnings, and later trace-recording failure does not roll back a committed transaction. No archive, trace, recovery, or canonical-document schema changes.

One deterministic archive contains a valid revision-one rename and matching version-one trace entry. An injected trace store captures that exact entry and rejects import. Open returns the source path plus the exact warning, emits the injected error once, exposes the revision-one artwork, accepts an immediate revision-two rename, and a fresh journal service recovers that exact dirty revision and file association.

The formal performance gate passes **1/1** at **882.7 MiB** RSS growth, with native save **133.72 ms** and PNG export **42.32 ms**, all below established budgets; this checkpoint adds no trace-store timing or exact peak-retention metric.
