**Material user-facing decision:** canonical artwork is authoritative over auxiliary trace-history availability. A local trace append failure must not make installed artwork appear unopened or block subsequent recoverable editing; instead, the user receives an addressed warning that history may be incomplete. Rolling back or withholding valid artwork because the separate trace store failed was rejected, as was silently swallowing the trace loss. This adds no retry UI, trace completeness or atomicity guarantee, cross-process/file-system durability, packaged warning presentation, broad native compatibility, Level 3, RC, or stable-v1 claim.

### Decision boundary — live state versus recovery-journal write failure

The adjacent audit changed no recovery behavior. Apply, undo, redo, and checkpoint restore install live state/history/dedupe/change state before awaiting journal append or compaction; an I/O rejection can therefore return a conflict or rejected promise after live state advanced but before ordinary publication. Moving the write earlier can race the separately ordered native-save/timer compaction paths and erase the just-written transaction with an older snapshot; rolling back afterward must also restore histories, dedupe, comparisons, checkpoints, and change feeds; reporting committed-with-degraded-recovery would weaken the P0 recovery promise. Existing product truth does not select among those semantics. A future checkpoint must choose one coherent durability and user-notification contract before changing this boundary; no directory-fsync, power-loss, or atomic live-plus-journal guarantee is claimed here.

## Source/headless same-document mutation-ordering checkpoint

Source/headless FND-04/FND-07/AGT-06/QA-08 evidence (2026-08-13): live transaction policy can await supervised image decode after reading the current document. The document service previously had no commit queue, so a later same-document request could commit first and the delayed transaction could then reduce from its stale object, reuse the same next revision, and overwrite that later work. Closing and adding a replacement with the same canonical ID while validation was pending could likewise let the old request install its former incarnation over the replacement. Undo, redo, and checkpoint restore had independent asynchronous journal boundaries rather than one revision-changing command order.

Canonical apply, undo, redo, and checkpoint-restore requests now enter one rejection-recovering queue per document after synchronous transaction schema/JSON-size admission. The command reads live state, dedupe, locks, and history only when its turn begins. Async transaction admission repeats the original incarnation and human-lock checks before reducer work; a closed/replaced document therefore returns an explicit conflict without canonical, history, change-feed, or recovery mutation. Different document IDs use different queues. Revision-bound native saves remain deliberately separate, capture their own invocation snapshots, and continue to permit later editing.

One deterministic decoder barrier starts an inline-asset commit, then invokes a same-document rename, undo, and redo. None settles before release, while a second document renames independently. Release produces exact revisions 1–4, retains both the asset and final name in live state and journal recovery, and records one contiguous change sequence. A second barrier queues checkpoint restore behind a pending asset commit: restore reaches revision 2, returns to the selected snapshot, and its automatic safety checkpoint contains the exact revision-1 asset branch. A third barrier force-closes the validating source, installs a same-ID replacement, and proves the delayed old request conflicts while replacement content, change feed, and recovered snapshot remain exact.

Focused pinned-Node-24 typecheck, targeted ESLint, and **2 files / 34 tests** pass. The complete safe gate passes portability for **369 tracked + 136 prospective paths**, TypeScript, full ESLint, and **181 files / 1,066 tests**. The formal performance gate passes **1/1** at **885.55 MiB** RSS growth, with native save **128.02 ms** and PNG export **40.8 ms**, all below established budgets; this checkpoint adds no mutation-queue-specific throughput or peak-retention metric. Ignored `test-results/performance-gate.json` remains local diagnostics. The non-certifying audit remains Level 3 `BLOCKED` **2** / stable v1 `BLOCKED` **5**, with **25** unfinished P0, **57** unclosed selected-v1 P1, and **259** dirty paths (**123 modified + 136 untracked**).

**Material user-facing decision:** invocation order is the authoritative order for revision-changing commands addressed to one open document. A later command waits rather than overtaking an earlier command during asynchronous admission; undo/redo observe that ordered result, and a stale old incarnation fails instead of being retargeted to a same-ID replacement. A global queue was rejected because unrelated documents should remain independent, and native saves were not folded into the mutation queue because their established revision-bound snapshot behavior intentionally permits continued edits. This checkpoint changes no reducer conflict/rebase rule, per-actor history semantics, client-operation dedupe, checkpoint data, journal/trace shape, visible playback/cancellation, save/open/import contract, file/interchange behavior, or infinite-map bounds/origin policy. It adds no cross-process ordering or locks, cancellation/coalescing, queue-persistence or persistent undo stack, directory-fsync/power-loss guarantee, packaged interaction evidence, Level 3, RC, or stable-v1 readiness.

## Source/headless document-identity admission checkpoint

Source/headless FND-06/FND-07/QA-08 evidence (2026-08-13): the open workspace stores canonical documents, histories, operation IDs, change feeds, checkpoints, trace association, save queues, and recovery state under the document ID. Native Open nevertheless checked only whether the exact requested path was already open before assigning the loaded document into that ID slot. A filesystem copy therefore carried the same canonical ID and could silently replace dirty live work plus its process-local state. Utility imports used the same single-document assignment in a loop, so a later collision could replace an open document after earlier siblings had already been admitted.

The existing exact-path behavior remains first: reopening that path activates the live entry and does not reload or clear unsaved state. A different native file whose canonical ID is already open now yields one addressed warning and is skipped before any workspace, checkpoint, trace, or recovery mutation; later noncolliding paths in the same request still open. Imported document results preflight every ID against the open workspace and the complete incoming set, then clone all documents before installing any of them. A successful set publishes once with its last document active; any collision rejects the complete set before a sibling is installed.

One deterministic native regression writes two valid `.aidraw` files with the same ID plus one independent sibling. It opens the first, commits an unsaved rename, proves exact-path reopen retains that dirty revision, refuses the copy with the exact warning, admits the sibling, and recovers the original dirty name/path/revision. A second regression first refuses a duplicated ID within one incoming set, then places one independent document before an open-workspace collision and proves both throws leave workspace revision, active state, document count, and recovered journal exact.

Focused pinned-Node-24 typecheck, targeted ESLint, and **2 files / 26 tests** pass. The complete safe gate passes portability for **369 tracked + 136 prospective paths**, TypeScript, full ESLint, and **181 files / 1,063 tests**. The formal performance gate passes **1/1** at **897.69 MiB** RSS growth, with native save **137.72 ms** and PNG export **48.75 ms**, all below established budgets; this checkpoint adds no dedicated timing or peak-RSS metric. Ignored `test-results/performance-gate.json` remains local diagnostics. The non-certifying audit remains Level 3 `BLOCKED` **2** / stable v1 `BLOCKED` **5**, with **25** unfinished P0, **57** unclosed selected-v1 P1, and **259** dirty paths (**123 modified + 136 untracked**).

**Material user-facing decision:** one canonical document ID may own only one open workspace entry. Preserve the existing entry and make the conflict explicit; users who intend to work on a copied file close the current entry before opening it. Automatic re-keying was rejected because IDs participate throughout document relationships, durable trace, checkpoints, recovery, collaboration, and future saves; merging, aliasing two paths, or treating the incoming file as a reload could silently invent or discard work. Exact string-path reopen remains the established idempotent exception; no realpath/symlink identity, clone-as-new command, schema or native-byte rewrite, cross-process coordination, broad `.aidraw` compatibility, packaged warning/dialog acceptance, Level 3, RC, or stable-v1 readiness is claimed.

## Latest source/headless revision-bound native-save checkpoint

Source/headless FND-06/FND-07/FND-09/ILL-18/QA-08 evidence (2026-08-13): native-save preview rendering already used an isolated document clone, but archive construction received the live document only after that asynchronous preview resolved. A transaction committed during rendering could therefore enter an archive whose preview represented the earlier revision, and save completion then set that newer live document clean even though the command had not captured or intentionally saved it. Concurrent saves also had no per-document completion order, and a completion after force-close could recreate recovery state for a no-longer-open document.

Each save now synchronously captures immutable artwork plus checkpoint records and a separate preview clone at invocation. Same-document requests enter one rejection-recovering queue in invocation order; different documents remain independent. Immediately before archive construction, the save reads durable trace history and retains only entries whose revision is no newer than the captured document. The native writer receives only that saved snapshot, so its validated archive and paint-cache materialization cannot observe later live mutations.

After atomic replacement, the completion acts on live state only while the original document incarnation is still open. It associates the destination and adopts only paint-cache prefixes whose stroke digest/geometry still match. If the live revision still equals the captured revision, the document becomes clean normally. If it advanced, the newer document remains dirty and is compacted into recovery with its new file association; the older archive remains exact. A completion after close/replacement writes the explicitly requested file but cannot mutate or resurrect the replacement document or its recovery journal.

A delayed trace-read regression commits a rename after save invocation, then proves the archive and preview retain revision zero, the newer revision-one trace is excluded, live state remains dirty at the saved path, and a fresh recovery journal restores that unsaved rename. A second barrier proves a queued save cannot enter preview while its predecessor is active and still succeeds after the first preview fails. A third force-closes and replaces the document at the same ID while preview is pending, then proves the stale completion leaves the replacement's name/path/dirty state and recovery snapshot exact.

Focused pinned-Node-24 typecheck, targeted ESLint, and **1 file / 20 tests** pass. The complete safe gate passes portability for **369 tracked + 136 prospective paths**, TypeScript, full ESLint, and **181 files / 1,061 tests**. The formal performance gate passes **1/1** at **803.42 MiB** RSS growth, with native save **127.7 ms** and PNG export **40.7 ms**, all below established budgets; it adds no concurrent-save timing or instantaneous peak-RSS assertion. Ignored `test-results/performance-gate.json` remains local diagnostics. The non-certifying audit remains Level 3 `BLOCKED` **2** / stable v1 `BLOCKED` **5**, with **25** unfinished P0, **57** unclosed selected-v1 P1, and **259** dirty paths (**123 modified + 136 untracked**).

**Material user-facing decision:** Save persists the exact revision present when that command was invoked; editing may continue, but later edits remain visibly dirty and recoverable rather than being silently folded into or cleared by the older save. Same-document saves complete in invocation order rather than completion-speed order. The successful older destination becomes the continuing document's file association even when later edits remain dirty; a closed/replaced incarnation is never rebound. Saving the latest state at eventual worker completion, blocking canonical editing, canceling/coalescing later saves, or marking a newer revision clean were rejected. Existing archive/checkpoint/trace schemas, destination normalization/overwrite policy, atomic writer, supervised preview admission, valid oversized 1×1 fallback, paint-cache digest policy, and cross-document concurrency stay exact. This does not guarantee a trace append not yet durable when the snapshot was captured, add directory fsync/power-loss semantics, prove packaged dialog/progress/error behavior, or establish Level 3, RC, or stable-v1 readiness.

## Latest source/headless application-store integrity checkpoint

Source/headless ILL-01/IO-09/QA-08 evidence (2026-08-13): reusable document presets and interchange reports used atomic file replacement, but each mutation first changed live state and each request persisted independently. If an older request paused before replacement while a newer request wrote a state containing both results, the delayed older replacement could still land last and discard the newer preset or report. A replacement failure could also leave a phantom update, deletion, or Activity report visible in memory even though restart restored the prior file.

Within one runtime store, every list/get/save/delete/record request now runs through one recoverable queue in invocation order. Save, delete, and record construct copied state, persist that complete value, then install it in memory only after success. A shared private JSON writer creates a unique same-directory temporary with exclusive mode-`0600` open, writes the complete indented value plus newline, flushes and closes it, atomically replaces the destination, and best-effort removes the temporary after a primary failure without masking that failure.

Deterministic barriers pause the first preset or report replacement, issue a second request, and prove the second replacement cannot begin early. After release, a fresh store retains both presets in call order and both newest-first reports in call order. Injected preset-update, preset-delete, and report-record replacement failures leave the prior file bytes and live values exact; failed temporaries are absent, POSIX destinations are mode `0600`, and the queue remains usable for subsequent reads.

Focused pinned-Node-24 typecheck, targeted ESLint, and **2 files / 7 tests** pass. The complete safe gate passes portability for **369 tracked + 136 prospective paths**, TypeScript, full ESLint, and **181 files / 1,058 tests**. The formal performance gate passes **1/1** at **899.92 MiB** RSS growth, with native save **126.51 ms** and PNG export **45.77 ms**, all below established budgets; it adds no store-specific timing or peak-RSS assertion. Ignored `test-results/performance-gate.json` remains local diagnostics. The non-certifying audit remains Level 3 `BLOCKED` **2** / stable v1 `BLOCKED` **5**, with **25** unfinished P0, **57** unclosed selected-v1 P1, and **259** dirty paths (**123 modified + 136 untracked**).

**Material user-facing decision:** requests to each store are process-locally linearizable in invocation order, and a mutation is successful and visible only after its replacement succeeds. Preserving existing version-one normalization/corrupt-file recovery was chosen over tightening legacy admission in this checkpoint; the 64-preset and 200-report caps, structured fidelity reasons, renderer/API contracts, and export behavior stay exact. Cross-process or multiple-store-instance locking, a file byte ceiling, directory-fsync/power-loss durability, packaged persistence/restart acceptance, comprehensive interchange loss accounting, broader PDF/PSD compatibility, Level 3, RC, and stable-v1 readiness remain unproved; ILL-01, IO-09, and QA-08 remain Working.

## Latest source/headless durable-batch integrity checkpoint

Source/headless PAR-09/QA-08 evidence (2026-08-13): a batch that had committed sequence 0 still accepted that same `clientOperationId` at sequence 1. The canonical document service correctly returned `duplicate`, but the batch manager treated that as sequence-1 success and advanced progress without a new canonical transaction. The ledger also trusted partial record shapes, retained old in-memory states when a later initialize saw a missing/corrupt file, mutated those live states before persistence succeeded, and wrote through one predictable temporary. A rejected replacement could therefore leave live batch progress different from the file and make an in-flight finish impossible to retry accurately.

Each new sequence now rejects a `clientOperationId` already committed by that batch; an exact request at its original older sequence still returns the established duplicate response. The version-one ledger root and each retained record require exact writer fields, bounded/consistent counts, strict actor/timestamp/status/error/in-flight shapes, aligned committed-ID arrays with unique committed operation IDs, and progress/status agreement. Invalid records are omitted independently; a missing or invalid root becomes an empty ledger and replaces the prior in-memory state after that empty replacement succeeds. Startup still reconciles one valid in-flight canonical commit from the durable trace before publishing recovered jobs.

Every state-changing start, resume, prepare, finish, and cancel operation clones the intended map first. A unique same-directory temporary is created exclusively at mode `0600`, receives the complete JSON plus newline, flushes and closes, then atomically replaces the ledger. Only after replacement succeeds does the manager install the map or publish job progress. Deterministic injected failures before prepare replacement leave the prior queued file/live state unchanged and allow the same prepare to succeed later; failures before finish replacement leave the running state unchanged and allow the same canonical result to finish later. Failed temporaries are removed. A nominally committed result without a transaction identity retains the prior sequence as retryable instead of producing a ledger hole.

Unit and production MCP coverage prove exact old-sequence retry, cross-sequence key-reuse conflict before revision change, later fresh-key completion across a host restart, byte-exact file preservation on prepare/finish replacement failures, retryability after both failures, private POSIX mode, temporary cleanup, strict per-record salvage, and stale-capability clearing on an invalid root. Focused pinned-Node-24 typecheck, targeted ESLint, and **2 files / 42 tests** pass. The complete safe gate passes portability for **369 tracked + 135 prospective paths**, TypeScript, full ESLint, and **181 files / 1,056 tests**. The formal gate passes **1/1** at **886.67 MiB** RSS, with native save at **132.93 ms** and PNG export at **41.3 ms**. The non-certifying audit remains Level 3 `BLOCKED` **2** / stable v1 `BLOCKED` **5**, with **25** unfinished P0, **57** unclosed selected-v1 P1, and **256** dirty paths (**121 modified + 135 untracked**).

**Material user-facing decision:** one new numbered batch step means one fresh batch-local idempotency key. The exact recorded key remains valid only to retry its original sequence; allowing it to occupy a later sequence was rejected because it reports progress for work the canonical model did not perform. Ledger state becomes visible only after replacement, and malformed records receive fail-closed per-record omission while an invalid root clears all resume capability. All-or-nothing root rejection for one bad member was rejected to preserve valid independent jobs; salvaging malformed member fields was rejected. This changes no resume-token disclosure (only its hash is stored), canonical drawing/journal/trace data, valid batch/restart/cancellation behavior, 500-batch or 10,000-transaction limits, document-wide or cross-batch idempotency, or infinite-map bounds/origin. It adds no ledger byte ceiling, directory flush/power-loss guarantee, packaged restart proof, Level 3, RC, or stable-v1 claim; PAR-09 remains Verified on source criteria and QA-08 remains Working.

## Latest source/headless MCP-control-state admission checkpoint

Source/headless IO-10/QA-04/QA-08 evidence (2026-08-13): the persisted folder-trust and preferred-port files were parsed with TypeScript assertions rather than runtime writer-shape checks. In particular, a JSON string in `folders` is iterable; an absolute path could therefore be processed one character at a time and install a broad root before startup continued. The host also accumulated persistent trust across repeated starts of the same process object, so corrupt replacement state could leave earlier authority alive. A numeric-looking preferred-port string could cross the candidate filter without being the integer writer contract expects.

Two pure parsers now accept only plain exact version-one records. Folder trust requires exactly `version` plus a unique array of absolute strings without NUL; preferred-port state requires exactly `version` plus an integer from **48200–48231**. Startup clears prior persistent authority, parses the complete folder list, canonicalizes it into a temporary set, rejects canonical aliases, and only then installs the complete result. Missing, malformed, stale, or contradictory state therefore grants no persistent folder authority. Valid state retains the same canonical paths and automatic non-overwrite behavior. Both writer paths now create a unique mode-`0600` temporary, write and flush the complete JSON, close it, and atomically replace the destination; primary failures clean the temporary. Persistent grants serialize through one queue, become active only after replacement succeeds, and the queue drains before engine stop completes.

The production MCP regression starts with a scalar absolute folder and proves an addressed export remains `waiting-for-user`; it then loads one valid writer-shaped folder and proves the same class of request is automatically queued with `trust: folder`; finally it corrupts the file and restarts the same host object, proving stale in-memory authority is cleared and a new request returns to `waiting-for-user`. Pure boundaries cover valid numeric port state, numeric strings, out-of-range/wrong-version/extra-field port records, scalar/relative/mixed/duplicate/extra-field trust records, and one valid absolute list.

Focused pinned-Node-24 typecheck, targeted ESLint, and the MCP contract pass at **1 file / 36 tests**. The complete safe gate passes portability for **369 tracked + 135 prospective paths**, TypeScript, full ESLint, and **181 files / 1,054 tests**. The formal gate passes **1/1** at **875.58 MiB** RSS, with native save at **127.99 ms** and PNG export at **42.28 ms**. The non-certifying audit remains Level 3 `BLOCKED` **2** / stable v1 `BLOCKED` **5**, with **25** unfinished P0, **57** unclosed selected-v1 P1, and **254 dirty paths** (**119 modified + 135 untracked**).

**Material user-facing decision:** persisted folder authority is admitted all-or-nothing from AIDraw's exact writer shape. Salvaging individual entries or retaining the previous in-memory set after a corrupt restart was rejected because either makes granted authority depend on malformed-state order or stale process history. Invalid preferred-port state falls back to the established numeric first-available scan rather than becoming a named-pipe/path listen target or blocking the MCP engine. This changes no valid persistent/session/launch trust, explicit approval, overwrite rule, realpath/symlink containment, port range, connection authentication, or file-operation authority. It adds no byte/count ceiling for settings, tamper signature, revocation UI, packaged restart proof, Level 3, RC, or stable-v1 claim; IO-10, QA-04, and QA-08 remain Working.

## Latest source/headless encrypted-credential admission checkpoint

Source/headless GEN-05/AGT-01/QA-08 evidence (2026-08-13): the hosted-provider credential parser already rejected a file above 256 KiB, but only after unrestricted `readFile` had allocated and decoded it. The separately encrypted localhost MCP bearer-token file also performed an unrestricted read before its existing corrupt-secret recovery. A damaged or deliberately enlarged user-data file could therefore allocate beyond the intended credential envelope before either path recovered.

Both encrypted files now use one shared **256 KiB** reader built on the handle-bound primitive. The actual opened regular-file handle owns the ceiling; the fixed allocation requests at most its admitted size plus one sentinel, and observable growth/shrinkage rejects before UTF-8 conversion, JSON, or `safeStorage`. An over-limit provider file retains the prior corrupt-file behavior: status exposes no stored credentials, no ciphertext is decrypted, and a later explicit provider rotation atomically writes normal valid state. An over-limit MCP token retains the prior corrupt-secret behavior: no oversized bytes are decrypted, a fresh random token is encrypted, and the file is replaced through its established path. Accepting a prefix or returning ciphertext was rejected.

Focused pinned-Node-24 typecheck, targeted ESLint, and the bounded-reader/MCP-credential/provider-credential/platform/generation-manager corpus pass at **5 files / 29 tests**. The complete safe gate passes portability for **369 tracked + 135 prospective paths**, TypeScript, full ESLint, and **181 files / 1,052 tests**. The unchanged formal gate passes **1/1** at **886.45 MiB** RSS, with native save at **143.55 ms** and PNG export at **42.01 ms**. The non-certifying audit remains Level 3 `BLOCKED` **2** / stable v1 `BLOCKED` **5**, with **25** unfinished P0, **57** unclosed selected-v1 P1, and **254 dirty paths** (**119 modified + 135 untracked**).

**Material user-facing decision:** reuse the provider credential file's established generous 256 KiB ceiling for the tiny encrypted MCP token, and retain each caller's existing corruption recovery instead of surfacing a new startup blocker. A rotated MCP token invalidates prior connection details exactly as any earlier corrupt-token recovery did; provider credentials remain absent until the user explicitly replaces them. This changes no plaintext/ciphertext exposure, secret schema, native DPAPI/Keychain/libsecret availability, provider removal/rotation semantics, MCP connection authority, path/permission/atomicity contract, or unrelated settings/report/journal/trace store. It does not provide a path-identity snapshot, detect every same-size rewrite, measure exact peak memory, prove packaged recovery/presentation, or establish Level 3, RC, or stable-v1 readiness.

## Latest source/headless bounded local-file read checkpoint

Source/headless FND-09/IO-01/IO-07/QA-08 evidence (2026-08-13): several existing input paths enforced a byte limit with `stat`, then called unrestricted `readFile`, and only checked the resulting buffer afterward. A file that grew between those operations could therefore force an allocation above the documented ceiling before AIDraw rejected it. This affected root/companion interchange imports, direct sprite-sheet selection/import, native archive open, human and approved-agent palette import, and human/approved-agent project-link relink.

One shared reader now performs a preliminary regular-file/limit check, opens with read-only nonblocking flags so a FIFO cannot stall before inspection, repeats regular-file and limit checks on the actual handle, and allocates exactly the handle-declared length plus one sentinel byte. It reads until EOF within that fixed allocation. The extra byte proves observable growth and is never returned; an observable short read proves shrinkage. Either case fails before parser/native decode/canonical construction. Exact-boundary files return their original bytes. Existing limits remain literal: palette **1 MiB**, retained sprite/project-link source **1,500,000 bytes**, structured import **16 MiB**, binary import **256 MiB**, and compressed native archive **512 MiB**. Existing realpath containment, source approval, parser/codec, source retention, canonical admission, and warning/failure behavior remain separate.

Pure tests admit an exact four-byte boundary, reject a directory, reject an opened file that crosses the cap without issuing a data read, prove growth requests at most declared size plus one, prove shrinkage rejection, and require handle closure on every path. Production source wiring requires import roots/companions, sprite sheets, native archives, both palette routes, and both project-link routes to use the primitive with no remaining `await readFile(` in those modules.

Focused pinned-Node-24 typecheck/ESLint and 6 files / 86 tests pass. The complete safe gate passes portability for **369 tracked + 134 prospective paths**, TypeScript, full ESLint, and **180 files / 1,048 tests**. The unchanged formal gate passes **1/1** at **863.11 MiB** RSS, with native save at **129.81 ms** and PNG export at **41.76 ms**. The non-certifying audit remains Level 3 `BLOCKED` **2** / stable v1 `BLOCKED` **5**, with **25** unfinished P0, **57** unclosed selected-v1 P1, and **251 dirty paths** (**117 modified + 134 untracked**).

**Material user-facing decision:** fail and ask for the bounded source again when its length is observed to change during the read. Silently accepting a prefix, rereading until stable, or streaming only some formats was rejected because each would change exact-source identity or create format-specific mutation semantics. This remains bounded whole-file admission: it does not stream parsers or ZIP output, reduce the existing permitted maximum buffer, provide a path-identity snapshot, detect every same-length concurrent content rewrite, change symlink/realpath authority, add file locking, measure exact peak memory, prove packaged dialogs/errors, or establish broad format compatibility, Level 3, RC, or stable-v1 readiness. FND-09, IO-01, IO-07, and QA-08 remain Working.

## Latest dependency-security and native-package checkpoint

Release/security evidence (2026-08-13): the fresh complete npm audit initially reported **34 package records (4 low / 29 high / 1 critical)** while the production runtime graph already reported zero. The reachable high/critical roots were all in rebuild/download, Forge prompt, ZIP extraction, or DMG tooling. The final graph reports **zero vulnerabilities** in both `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` and the fail-closed complete audit with production, development, optional, and peer dependencies explicitly included. The exact four includes override ambient npm `omit` configuration; pinned-npm subprocess coverage proves each npm-supported ambient omit category resolves to an empty effective omit set, and a live complete audit under ambient `omit=dev` also reports zero. `npm audit signatures` verifies **641 registry signatures** and **141 attestations**.

Electron is now pinned to **43.4.0**, the latest published 43.x patch at review time, and `verify-package.mjs` rejects any different packaged runtime version. Forge stays at **7.11.2** with Packager **18.4.4**: Forge 7 still adapts positional callback hooks and Packager 18 promisifies them, while Packager 20's object/promise hooks are not a compatible override. The exact installed-source regression pins that adapter, the Packager 18 hook runner, its CommonJS/default-export bridge, and its `extract(zipPath, { dir })` call. Only the extractor implementation is substituted with Electron's signed/provenance-published drop-in `@electron-internal/extract-zip@1.0.5`. Rebuild **4.2.0**, tar **7.5.22**, and tmp **0.2.7** remove the old rebuild/prompt chains.

The unpatched Forge DMG-maker chain is absent. Its replacement is a macOS-only `MakerBase` implementation that validates artifact leaves, copies the completed app through absolute `/usr/bin/ditto`, adds the ordinary Applications symlink, creates a compressed image through absolute `/usr/bin/hdiutil`, verifies the image before returning, and removes partial output on any failure. The release and CI workflows now call one `security:verify` gate instead of a production-only audit. Manifest/lock policy rejects version drift, the former vulnerable build roots, a non-Electron extractor alias, or any external non-link lock entry without an exact HTTPS `registry.npmjs.org` tarball and SHA-512 integrity. The only exceptions are the explicitly allowed `packages/canvas` and `packages/core` workspace records and their links; the current graph validates **701** external entries.

Focused pinned-Node-24 compatibility/release coverage after the acceptance correction passes **3 files / 20 tests**, plus typecheck and targeted ESLint. The prior complete safe gate passed portability for **369 tracked + 153 prospective paths**, TypeScript, full ESLint, and **189 files / 1,115 tests** before the two focused policy tests were added. The prior formal performance gate passed **1/1** at **966.19 MiB** RSS growth; native save was **127.31 ms** and PNG export **41.56 ms**, with every established metric below budget. Because the correction changes neither the lock nor packaging implementation, packaging, full-suite, and performance work were not repeated. Ignored `test-results/performance-gate.json` remains local diagnostics.

A real pinned-Node-24 Apple Silicon make completed Forge packaging, native-dependency preparation, ad-hoc signing, DMG creation, and ZIP creation. Package verification reports Electron **43.4.0**, Forge **7.11.2**, Packager **18.4.4**, extractor **1.0.5**, the expected fuses/security surface, and no build-only dependency inside the shipped ASAR. Independent `hdiutil verify` reports the final 145,861,414-byte DMG valid; `unzip -tq` reports the 142,867,837-byte ZIP valid. Local checksum output records DMG SHA-256 `6670cc79d507e1223a20a0c7208c719bb559a758e45c3edc1188a2b8ed70eb31` and ZIP SHA-256 `463762520e71ab9973515bd4701e4f643adba5453e9d4b9faa73b5bd6f0fdd4c`. Sandboxed `hdiutil create` first failed with host-level “device not configured”; the unchanged make succeeded through the required unsandboxed macOS disk-image boundary. These ignored artifacts are local verification, not retained release evidence.

**Material release decision:** preserve the supported Forge 7/Packager 18 hook contract and replace only the vulnerable extractor instead of forcing Packager 20 or accepting npm's Forge 7-to-6 downgrade. Replace the advisory-blocked appdmg stack with a deliberately plain native maker rather than waiving an unpatched build advisory. Users receive the current Electron 43.4.0 runtime and an ordinary app-plus-Applications-link DMG; custom DMG backgrounds/layout and automatic signing were rejected as unrelated to this security boundary. No source-format behavior or compatibility promise changed.

This checkpoint does not prove Windows/Linux makers with the new lock, mount and inspect the final DMG contents independently, create a clean provenance record, compare independent builds, sign/notarize/staple, establish Gatekeeper acceptance, run hosted CI, create independent Level 3 evidence, or establish RC/stable-v1 readiness. REL-03, REL-04, and REL-07 retain their existing non-final statuses.

## Latest source/headless stale-editor recovery checkpoint

Source/headless FND-05 evidence (2026-08-14): a live single-instance owner kept one `BrowserWindow` reference and considered every non-destroyed native shell usable. `createWindow()` therefore showed and focused that shell without checking whether its renderer contents were destroyed or crashed, and no `render-process-gone` or main-frame load-failure handler retired it. This is a deterministic contract matching the narrow observation that one older long-running owner showed a blank window with no renderer helper while a fresh isolated current package worked. It does not prove that the observed process ran this exact source, used the same profile, or failed for this reason.

One Electron-independent lifecycle coordinator now owns editor-window attachment while `EngineRuntime` remains separate. The main frame must load successfully before the editor is reported attached or visible playback is enabled. An initial `loadURL` rejection, renderer-process exit, later non-aborted main-frame load failure, or explicit show that finds destroyed/crashed renderer contents removes that renderer from the trusted IPC identity, marks the engine headless, logs a bounded reason, and retires only the stale shell. A terminal renderer event wins a race with the original load promise, so recovery does not wait forever merely because that promise never settles. One replacement is admitted only after native-shell destruction is confirmed; a destroy/confirmation exception leaves the stale shell quarantined and blocks a second shell until a later close or explicit retirement retry. Create, event-binding, load, health, and reveal exceptions remain inside the same boundary.

Confirmed editor detach clears all process-local human gesture locks because AIDraw has one editor window and a departed renderer cannot complete or release them; renderer IPC also rejects new human-lock admission while attachment is false. It does not cancel a canonical human transaction already admitted by the browser process; canonical documents, revisions, histories, recovery, jobs, and agent presence otherwise remain unchanged. A full-document reload reports the renderer document detached, clears its gesture locks, and disables visual playback until `did-finish-load`; a healthy `did-stop-loading` after an `ERR_ABORTED` replacement navigation reattaches the same trusted shell rather than rebuilding it. Same-document navigation does not detach. A tentative close keeps the same trusted renderer identity and locks until Electron confirms closure or cancellation, so an in-flight release cannot be rejected and stranded merely because the close is later cancelled. Main-process `defaultPrevented` and renderer `will-prevent-unload` cancellation retain the same healthy renderer, including recovery if its process disappeared during the attempted close; confirmed close performs the real detach. Concurrent explicit shows coalesce onto one load, retain a distinct show intent if the joined attempt closes or an automatic replacement fails, and wait across a closing shell before reattach. A renderer or load failure after a show begins that wait wakes the explicit intent; without such an intent, an ordinary confirmed close still does not reopen. One explicit show permits one automatic replacement; a consecutive replacement failure remains headless until another explicit launch. Pending/completed graceful shutdown suppresses opening, including an in-flight load; failed shutdown resumes only the retained interrupted retry.

Deterministic tests cover pre-ready attachment truth; full-document reload and aborted-reload attachment transitions; healthy minimized reuse; initial load rejection; a renderer terminal event racing a never-settling load; create/bind/reveal/destroy exceptions; duplicate-shell prevention; renderer-gone replacement; missed crashed-shell discovery; later main-frame failure; bounded retry; stale close events; concurrent shows during load, automatic failure, and close, including failure after a close wait begins; healthy and crashed cancelled-close paths; intentional close; shutdown gating, in-flight suppression, failed-shutdown resumption, and exact non-aborted main-frame classification. Document-service regressions prove an overlapping agent transaction is locked during a live gesture, confirmed detach clears occupancy before a fresh agent commit, and an already admitted canonical human transaction still completes afterward. Production-source assertions bind Electron navigation, failure, and close-cancellation events. Existing FND-07 engine stop/retry tests, UX-05 renderer-event recovery/diagnostics, renderer sender/origin security, packaged security markers, and RC parsing remain green. Pinned-Node-24 typecheck, targeted ESLint, and the focused **9 files / 92 tests** pass. The complete safe gate passes portability for **522 tracked + 2 untracked paths**, TypeScript, full ESLint, and **190 files / 1,145 tests**. The non-certifying audit parses the reconciled **146-item** tracker and remains Level 3 `BLOCKED` **2** / stable v1 `BLOCKED` **5**, with **28** P0 items not Verified, **58** selected-v1 P1 items not closed, and this exact **11-path** source checkpoint dirty. No performance gate was rerun because this lifecycle/lock cleanup adds no measured rendering, storage, scheduling, or other measured hot path.

**Material user-facing decision:** replace only a confirmed failed editor shell once, release its transient gesture locks only on confirmed detach, and keep the canonical engine/profile untouched. A lock whose renderer has departed cannot represent active human occupancy; retaining it indefinitely was rejected because it permanently blocks overlapping agents. Clearing a still-live renderer's locks on a merely attempted close was also rejected because a cancelled close must not silently surrender human priority. Clearing caches or user data, restarting the engine, discarding documents, killing an owner, retaining an abandoned gesture, cancelling already admitted canonical work, admitting a replacement before old-shell retirement, or entering an unbounded reload loop was rejected. A second consecutive failure stays headless and can be retried by explicitly opening AIDraw again. This preserves the existing clean headless attach/close/reattach path and keeps FND-07 persisted-document recovery plus UX-05 in-renderer event containment distinct.

That earlier source checkpoint did not define alive-but-unresponsive confirmation. Synchronized commit `a3e4de587ec53fd746a25573b5cf257e1f4202fd` adds the bounded policy below without diagnosing an already-running older executable, recovering a browser process that cannot receive the second-instance signal, clearing/repairing a profile, or itself supplying native packaged evidence. The separate exact-package crash r2 closes only fresh-profile crash/replacement/close-show/relaunch acceptance; the later alive-hang r5 section records its distinct narrow macOS/arm64 current-package PASS. FND-05 remains Working/P0 because the historical/default-profile cause, renderer-local unsubmitted work, cache/profile repair, broader platforms, Level 3, RC, and stable-v1 readiness remain open.

### Current alive-but-unresponsive editor-shell confirmation

Pinned Electron **43.4.0** declares paired `BrowserWindow` `unresponsive` and `responsive` events. Production now starts one non-extending **10,000 ms** unref'ed main-process timer after Electron emits `unresponsive`; it adds no renderer polling, heartbeat IPC, environment-gated hook, or profile mutation. A matching `responsive` event inside that grace cancels confirmation and retains the exact shell, attachment truth, transient gesture occupancy, and automatic-retry allowance. Repeated `unresponsive` signals do not extend the deadline, and a cancelled timer callback is state-checked so stale work from a closed or replaced shell cannot affect its successor.

Expiry confirms the persistent hang and enters the existing terminal-shell path: trusted current-window identity is removed, editor attachment and visual playback become false, transient human gesture locks clear through `DocumentService.setEditorAttached(false)`, and native-shell retirement must be confirmed before at most one replacement attaches to the same canonical engine. Canonical documents, revisions, history, jobs, agent state, and already admitted canonical human transactions remain main-process owned and are not reset or cancelled. A consecutive automatic replacement failure remains headless until another explicit show.

A close that emits `closed` during the grace cancels the timer and stays closed. A tentative close still unresolved after Electron's unresponsive signal plus the full grace is not a confirmed user close: its waiter is released and the unusable shell is retired, so failed/cancelled close cannot suppress recovery indefinitely. Responsiveness before expiry plus close cancellation retains the same shell. Main-frame reload may be transiently responsive and reattach the same shell, while a persistent reload hang follows terminal recovery. A persistent hang during pending/completed shutdown retires the shell but cannot open a replacement; a failed shutdown resumes only the retained bounded retry.

Deterministic lifecycle coverage proves the exact grace, repeated events, responsive cancellation, stale callback rejection, persistent replacement and one-retry limit, a never-settling initial load with concurrent shows, responsive and persistent tentative-close paths, intentional close, responsive and persistent full-document reload, and shutdown failure/resumption. Existing document-service coverage separately proves detach clears transient occupancy while preserving a canonical human transaction already admitted before detach. Production-source assertions bind the exact Electron 43 window events. This evidence does not launch Electron, reproduce the historical protected default-profile instance, prove Electron's platform-specific event timing, simulate a native CPU/GPU hang, or establish packaged recovery, cache/profile repair, broad platforms, RC, or stable-v1 readiness.

That synchronized **six-path** source checkpoint passed pinned Node **v24.19.0** typecheck, targeted ESLint, the focused lifecycle/document-service/shutdown/FND-05/package-runtime/RC corpus at **6 files / 86 tests**, and a complete safe gate at portability **536 tracked + 0 untracked paths**, TypeScript, full ESLint, and **193 files / 1,177 tests**. Its clean post-push non-certifying RC audit parsed **146 items**, remained Level 3 `BLOCKED` by **1** issue and stable v1 `BLOCKED` by **4**, and retained **28** unfinished P0 rows plus **58** selected-v1 P1 rows not closed. The controller-only preparation below changes no production byte and earns no native result.

Pinned package metadata identifies Electron **43.4.0**, Chromium **150.0.7871.224**, and Playwright **1.62.1**. Electron's declarations expose `WebContents` `unresponsive`/`responsive`; the installed framework retains the input-ACK and `WebContentsImpl::RendererUnresponsive` source symbols used in r2/r3. Chromium primary source establishes the missing r3 seam: `RenderInputRouter` uses a one-shot in-flight input-event ACK timer; `WebContentsImpl::RendererUnresponsive` returns immediately when `ShouldIgnoreUnresponsiveRenderer()` is true; that predicate returns `DevToolsAgentHost::IsDebuggerAttached(this)` because an apparent hang may be a breakpoint. The restarter is not invoked on that early return, and the ACK timer restarts only through explicit responsive/ACK paths—not merely because a debugger later detaches. The exact 43.4.0 source checkout is not vendored, so matching Chromium 150 to current primary source is a version-specific inference corroborated by the pinned binary's source paths/symbols and r3's result, not a new native observation.

Installed Playwright code makes both attachment lifetime and r4 command settlement exact for this controller. `connectOverCDP` enables root `Target.setAutoAttach`, each page session also auto-attaches, and `newCDPSession(page)` adds the explicit target session. R3 retained all of them through the 35-second wait, so it was self-suppressing even though F24 was ACK-pending. For a browser obtained through `connectOverCDP`, public `browser.close()` is wired to the WebSocket transport's `closeAndWait`; the separate launched-browser graceful-close path is the one that sends protocol `Browser.close`. In pinned Playwright **1.62.1**, Chromium transport `_onClose` calls only `rootSession.dispose()`. The target `CDPSession` owns a child `CRSession` with its own callback map; that child is normally disposed by a target-detached event, but no such event can arrive after transport close. The server dispatcher and client channel both await that unresolved child send with `kNoTimeout`, while browser disconnection completes independently. This deterministic installed-source path matches r4's pending F24 promise after a proven disconnect. Source tests bind every link in that root-versus-child chain and continue to reject Playwright private fields or protocol owner-close commands.

The corrected external controller retains the initial proof, then uses one synchronous bounded **65,000 ms renderer-only CDP stall** and exactly one target-local `Input.dispatchKeyEvent` raw-key event for benign **F24** on the already identified stalled renderer. After the **2,000 ms** pending/alive/owner/MCP/canonical-state/lock proof and at most **1,000 ms** of input-admission observation, it closes the entire public Playwright `connectOverCDP` transport within **2,000 ms**. Thus every Playwright auto-attach and the explicit target session must be gone by a derived **5,000 ms** deadline, well before the conservative 15-second ACK budget. Only after independent full-detach, marker-absence, owner-alive, same-owner MCP, and lock-continuity proof does it observe the input and stall command promises concurrently for one bounded **2,000 ms** post-detach interval. A promise that remains pending becomes the explicit `pending-after-debugger-detach` disposition; the controller does not await it again. It then preserves marker absence through the 10-second no-replacement floor without a debugger and requires the exact product stderr confirmation before any reconnect. It changes neither physical keyboard nor canonical application state and adds no production IPC/preload/environment hook, `Page.crash`, reload, polling, profile repair, engine restart, OS automation, or force cleanup.

The 65-second stall bound is derived rather than padded arbitrarily: 5 seconds for observation, admission, and complete debugger detach; 35 seconds for a conservative 15-second input-ACK budget plus AIDraw's fixed non-extending 10-second grace plus 10 seconds of scheduling margin; 15 seconds for replacement-process admission and bounded same-endpoint reconnect; and a final 10-second safety margin. Input submission must resolve as browser-admitted or remain ACK-pending after one second; an immediate rejection fails. After independently proven detach, each command may resolve only in its allowed state, reject only with a reviewed public-disconnect error, or remain pending for the exact bounded 2,000 ms observation. Missing/unknown observations, a different pending interval, a resolved stall, and unrelated rejections fail closed. Both observations start together, so neither child promise can hang the controller or sequentially consume the debugger-free budget. ## Latest source/headless professional editor-density checkpoint

UX-01 source evidence (2026-08-14) replaces the primary editor's scattered tiny dimensions with one bounded professional-density contract. BrowserWindow requests a 1,520×940 default renderer content viewport with Electron `useContentSize` semantics. Electron's minimum-size API remains an outer-window contract, so AIDraw measures the newly created outer/content delta and adds that native frame inset to the outer minimum; enforced renderer content remains 980×640. On macOS, `titleBarStyle: hiddenInset` keeps native traffic lights but overlays them into AIDraw's existing 56 px top bar instead of adding another vertical title-bar band. The renderer URL explicitly selects that platform style, CSS reserves 84 px for traffic lights, the top bar is draggable, and interactive descendants are no-drag. Other platforms retain their ordinary native title bars. The CSS compact breakpoint is the renderer's 1,120 px content width. Shared values define 12/11/10 px primary text, 38/32 px primary/secondary targets, 19–21 px primary icons, a 56 px top bar, 48 px context bar, 38 px status bar, 62 px tool rail, and 318/286 px regular/compact inspector. This is selective shell density, not a page transform, browser zoom, whole-UI scale, or frameless custom-window replacement.

At the declared 980×640 minimum content viewport, the compact rail and inspector leave a deterministic 632×498 base canvas floor; the 138 px sprite timeline leaves 360 px of visible canvas height. PixelCanvas subtracts `EDITOR_DENSITY.timelineHeight` from its observed container before sizing the drawable canvas, while both CSS timeline declarations and the cel-exposure/onion panel offsets consume `--timeline-height`; coordinate mapping therefore uses only the region above the timeline. Document tabs, tools, context controls, inspector content, pixel controls, frames, and tags preserve their existing axis-specific scrolling instead of clipping or silently hiding critical controls. Compact mode removes the redundant brand wordmark and agent-count text only; the agent activity button now has its own accessible name. A short-height rule reduces only vertical rail spacing while retaining the primary target size. The 38/32 px values were chosen as a bounded desktop-editor baseline rather than a 44 px touch layout, which would consume disproportionate canvas space; touch, 200% text, and hardware acceptance remain separate.

Pure contract coverage binds requested BrowserWindow content dimensions and the inset-derived native minimum to shared constants; requires macOS `hiddenInset` rather than `enableLargerThanScreen`; binds the platform URL marker, traffic-light reservation, drag/no-drag CSS, root 100%/hidden-overflow shell, every density token, and the content-width compact breakpoint; calculates both minimum canvas floors; proves primary control sizing; and retains progressive-disclosure overflow. A headless work-area regression records the r2 host's read-only 1,920×960 visible frame, r2's observed 1,520×928 content, the inferred 32 px separate title bar, and the unsafe 972 px decorated height that `enableLargerThanScreen` would require. It proves both 1,520×940 default content and 980×640 minimum content fit the host work area when the title bar is inset. This evidence does not establish smaller work areas or arbitrary display/menu/dock configurations.

**Material user-facing decision:** enlarge and regularize the always-used editor chrome while preserving canvas area through compact columns and local scrolling. Persisted panel arrangement, hidden-panel policy, shortcut remapping, and new workspace disclosure behavior were rejected as a separate UX-01/08 product slice rather than invented here. The source checkpoint alone did not prove visual comfort, absence of clipping, pointer accuracy, keyboard order, screen-reader behavior, 200%-text reflow, high-DPI/mixed-monitor behavior, tablet/touchpad input, or packaged/native rendering. The immutable r5 run below now closes only its named supported-viewport, reachability, workflow, timeline, screenshot, network, credential, and cleanup assertions. UX-01 remains Working for the separate customizable-workspace, progressive-disclosure, accessibility, hardware/display, and broader-polish exit criteria; UX-09 and UX-10 retain their distinct evidence boundaries.

### UX-01 native window-chrome preparation, consumed r1/r2/r3 controller failures, and corrected source model

`UX-01-WINDOW-CHROME exact package preserves native traffic lights and drag/no-drag routing` has three consumed immutable macOS/arm64 results but no native PASS. R1 `20260814t092808z-7ff773c-r1` ran once and failed **1/1 in 3.2 seconds** at `native-window-identity` because the controller directly compared Quartz-native absolute `x` with Blink `screenX`; its failure record lacked raw values. Fresh r2 `20260814t102354z-f7e6d0c-r2` also ran exactly once. Its no-prompt permission preflight and action-time recheck passed; exact owner PID 14422, renderer PID 14431, and one on-screen layer-zero Quartz window ID 7029 were admitted. It then exited **1** with **1/1 failed in 2.6 seconds** before input: Quartz reported `203,32 1514×936`, while trusted Blink reported `200,30 1520×940` at DPR 1 with root/body client and scroll geometry `1520×940`. The width check failed first. Fresh r3 `20260814t112230z-ff44905-r3` admitted exact owner PID 22702, one renderer, and one on-screen layer-zero Quartz window ID 7030. Its accepted pair was again Quartz `203,32 1514×936` and Blink `200,30 1520×940` at DPR 1 with live insets `3/2/3/2`. It then exited **1** at `interactive-no-drag-click` before input: the controller completed asynchronous renderer target observation but still passed its earlier native-bounds snapshot to the driver's strict action-time reinspection, which found a bounds change and refused admission. R3 retained neither the new bounds nor the physical cause, so it proves a controller TOCTOU/evidence defect but cannot distinguish first-show settling from another native move. No CoreGraphics input was posted in any run. R2 owner 14422 and graceful signal helper 14438 exited 0 with zero survivors; r3 also cleaned up gracefully with zero survivors. R1/r2/r3 are controller/evidence-model failures, not product traffic-light/drag behavior, privacy completion, ordinary-green fullscreen behavior, or native acceptance. All three packages, profiles, private failure/cleanup/Playwright records, compiled drivers, and ordinary encrypted profile credential records are immutable and non-reusable; credential contents remain unopened.

The corrected source model uses only pinned primary contracts. Apple documents `kCGWindowBounds` in Quartz screen space with the origin at the upper-left of the main display, but does not promise equality with AppKit's decorated frame. Electron v43.4.0 `NativeWindowMac::GetBounds` reads `NSWindow.frame`, preserves its width/height, and flips its y origin against the primary screen. Chromium 150.0.7871.224 `LocalDOMWindow` returns the embedder's `RootWindowRect` for `screenX`/`screenY`/`outerWidth`/`outerHeight`; CSSOM defines those values in CSS pixels. R2's `−6×−4` size delta and symmetric `3×2` visible inset therefore invalidate both direct origin and size equality.

Raw native and renderer records are retained before every assertion. On the declared DPR-1/main-display route, the controller dynamically requires the on-screen Quartz visible rectangle to be wholly contained by the Electron-frame-derived Blink root rectangle, requires renderer content/root/body geometry to cover that nominal frame without overflow, and records all four observed edge insets. It never hard-codes r2's `3×2` relation or widens an equality tolerance. Before interaction it requires three consecutive paired native/renderer observations at 100 ms intervals within a five-second ceiling, with exact window identity, independent per-space stationarity, and a stable decorated-frame relation. After each renderer or fixed native target has been observed and immediately before every native post, it captures a fresh paired action-ready snapshot and requires it to remain stationary against the last accepted state. That fresh relation and native bounds drive mapping and admission; movement between the paired snapshot and post still fails in the driver's exact action-time reinspection. A refusal now retains redaction-safe expected and actual bounds, while a controller-side drift retains both prior and current raw geometry. A nominal-frame point maps to Quartz-local input by subtracting the currently observed leading inset; the complete mapped two-pixel action neighborhood must remain inside the current Quartz bounds. Renderer-derived drag/no-drag targets additionally require a complete 0.5-CSS-px hit-test neighborhood inside the intended app region before mapping. Native traffic-light targets remain derived only from Electron's declared frame margin plus local AppKit standard-button metrics, then use the same dynamic frame-to-Quartz mapping. No-drag actions must remain stationary independently in Quartz and Blink; intended drag must move in the requested direction and bounded distance independently in both spaces without resizing, and the observed decorated-frame relation must remain stable within the existing two-pixel action bound. A second/ambiguous window, DPR other than 1, overflow, non-contained/fractional relation, incomplete hit neighborhood, out-of-window mapping, identity drift, pre-action movement, driver-time movement, or per-space movement mismatch fails closed.

A future retry requires one wholly fresh single-use package generation plus matching absent `test-results/retained/aidraw-e2e-ux01-window-chrome-<run-id>` profile and `test-results/retained-failures/ux01-window-chrome-native-<run-id>` failure root, exact launcher/ASAR SHA-256 declarations, macOS/arm64, the dedicated wrapper, and one exact selector. Discovery runs with synthetic roots absent and lists exactly that one case without launching or creating a root.

The product contract keeps native `hiddenInset` controls and explicitly restates Electron 43.4.0's pinned standard-button left-top margin at **12×11 px** inside the existing **84 px** traffic-light reserve. That is the official `hiddenInset` default consumed by Electron's `WindowButtonsProxy`, so the checkpoint makes the target deterministic without moving the user-visible controls; all CSS drag/no-drag geometry continues to share the same reserve. The corrected controller must bind the spawned package PID, one renderer process/page, and one layer-zero Quartz window ID before each action. It derives close/minimize/zoom centers from a local unshown AppKit standard-window probe, dynamically maps those nominal-frame points into the current Quartz visible bounds, then proves: a native click and a bounded in-control drag on the named All open documents no-drag descendant do not move the window and leave that control keyboard-usable; a drag beginning only on a renderer-confirmed non-interactive app-region neighborhood moves the same window without changing content size; an **Option-click** on the standard green button changes to AppKit's standard zoom geometry, stays inside the renderer-reported work area instead of entering fullscreen, and a second exact-bound Option-click restores the prior geometry; minimize makes that window off-screen and same-profile `--show` restores it; close retires the window/renderer while preserving the owner, and same-profile `--show` admits exactly one distinct window and renderer. Every stage fails closed on a second/ambiguous window, PID/window/current-native-bounds drift, missing standard controls, invalid decorated-frame containment, incomplete hit-test coverage, unsafe mapped target, independent movement/relation failure, an out-of-reserve target, unexpected process shape, out-of-work-area zoom, or incomplete behavior.

The run-private Objective-C driver is compiled only by the dedicated wrapper into the mode-0700 failure root. Before Playwright can launch the exact package—and before the test can create its profile—the wrapper invokes the helper's PID-free, no-prompt `CGPreflightPostEventAccess` command and fails closed unless it returns true. The helper rechecks that permission immediately before every exact-PID action. It otherwise uses public `CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo`, exact owner/window identifiers, AppKit standard-button geometry, modifier-bearing `CGEventSetFlags`, and `CGEventPostToPid`; it never calls `CGRequestPostEventAccess`. The route uses no Accessibility API, AppleScript, screen capture, global `CGEventPost`, signal/force action, private Playwright/CDP field, Browser-domain window mutation, or production IPC/preload test hook. This is deliberately external exact-PID input, not Computer Use or a reusable desktop-control surface, and it requires a separate native authority before one later execution.

The wrapper rejects credential-capable Playwright reporter settings, uses list-only reporting with trace/video/automatic screenshots off, creates only private 0700/0600 records, and passes an explicit non-provider child environment to the compiler, controller, and package. The controller neither requests nor reads a local MCP credential and does not pass provider credentials to the package. Remote debugging is loopback-only; background networking is disabled; external renderer requests are directly observed while attached; provider-generation and forbidden-network sentinels must stay absent. Cleanup is graceful-only through the exact same-profile `--quit-engine`; surviving owned processes are a blocker and are never force-killed. PASS/failure evidence records exact package/driver hashes, owner/renderer/window identities, native/renderer geometry, stage results, request count, and cleanup without retaining a credential-shaped value.

This exact **eight-path** source/headless correction passes strict Objective-C syntax validation without producing a helper binary, pinned Node **v24.19.0** typecheck, targeted ESLint, and the focused window-chrome/density/package-policy/FND-05/RC corpus at **5 files / 52 tests**. Its final complete safe component gate passes portability for **548 tracked + 0 untracked paths**, TypeScript, full ESLint, and **195 files / 1,207 tests**. The non-certifying audit still parses **146 items**, reports Level 3 `BLOCKED` by **2** and stable v1 `BLOCKED` by **5** because the exact source checkpoint is dirty, and retains the clean synchronized base's **1 / 4** result. No performance, package/build/reseal, Electron/helper/native input, Computer Use, provider/network/credential, retained-evidence, staging, commit, or push action is part of this correction.

The correction preserves explicit nonclaims for CoreGraphics permission availability on a future run host, ordinary green-click fullscreen behavior, arbitrary macOS/Electron versions, Windows/Linux chrome, smaller-than-supported work areas, Accessibility/Computer Use behavior, customizable workspaces/progressive-disclosure completion, 200% text/assistive technology, tablet/touchpad input, high-DPI/mixed displays, broad polish, RC, and stable v1. UX-01 remains **Working/P0** until a separately authorized fresh securely fused exact package completes the route once and its private evidence is independently audited.

## Failure severity and reruns

| Severity | Meaning | Gate effect |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Blocker | Cannot launch/attach/test, persistent white screen, data loss/corruption, unsafe authority bypass, or result cannot be trusted | Fails every level immediately. |
| P0 | Core workflow, security boundary, recovery, coordinate correctness, or release artifact failure | Fails every formal level. |
| P1 | Major advertised workflow is broken without a safe practical workaround | Fails Level 2/3; may fail Level 1 when in changed scope. |
| P2 | Bounded defect with a clear workaround | Record; release requires explicit disposition. |
| P3 | Cosmetic/polish issue | Record and triage. |

After a fix, spawn a **fresh Luna/high task**. A rerun may target the failed case plus the level's smoke core, but a release candidate must receive a fresh complete Level 3 run.

## Tester prompt

Use [testing/LUNA_HIGH_PROMPT.md](testing/LUNA_HIGH_PROMPT.md) as the canonical task prompt. Substitute the level, subject/build identity, and report run ID. Do not remove its Computer Use, isolation, evidence, or no-source-edits requirements.
